Transposing the regularisation to the kernels of the chaos expansion we can write \(K^{\tau} (X_{\varepsilon}) = K_{\varepsilon}^{\tau} (X)\) and now it is easy to check that \(K_{\varepsilon}^{\tau} (X) \rightarrow K^{\tau} (X)\) in probability (as done systematically in [4],[18]). We can then conclude that \(K^{\tau} (Y_{\varepsilon}) \rightarrow K^{\tau} (X)\) and therefore \(Y^{\tau}_{\varepsilon} \rightarrow Y^{\tau} (\lambda)\) in law for every \(\tau\), since from Assumption 1 we have immediately \(f_{\tau} (\lambda_{\varepsilon}) \rightarrow f_{\tau} (\lambda)\).

Let us give some more details on how to prove the decomposition (28) and the bounds (30) and (31). As seen in Section 3.1 we have \(Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta} = \langle \xi, h_{\zeta} \rangle\) in law for \(\zeta = (t, x) \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\) and this gives

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D}^n \tilde{F}^{(m)}_{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta}) = \tilde{F}^{(m + n)}_{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta}) h^{\otimes n}_{\zeta} .\)

We define for \(m \in \mathbb{N}\), \(\zeta \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\):

\(\displaystyle \Phi_{\zeta}^{[m]} := \varepsilon^{\frac{m - 3}{2}} \tilde{F}_{\varepsilon}^{ (m)} (\varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta}) \) (32)

Note that the term \(\Phi^{[m]}\) above is not the \(m\)-th derivative of some function \(\Phi\) (we use the square parenthesis notation to emphasize this fact). It easy to see from (32) that \(\mathrm{D}^k \Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta} = \Phi^{[m + k]}_{\zeta} h_{\zeta}^{\otimes k}\). Therefore, the partial chaos expansion (82) takes a more explicit form when applied to \(\Phi_{\zeta}^{[m]}\)\(\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Phi_{\zeta}^{[m]} & = & \sum_{k = 0}^{n - 1} \frac{\mathbb{E} (\Phi^{[m + k]}_{\zeta})}{k!} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^k_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \delta^n (G_{[1]}^{[n]} \Phi^{[m + n]}_{\zeta} h_{\zeta}^{\otimes n})\\ & = & \sum_{k = 0}^{n - 1} \varepsilon^{(m + k - 3) / 2} \frac{(m + k) !}{k!} \tilde{f}_{m + k, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^k_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \delta^n (G_{[1]}^{[n]} \Phi^{[m + n]}_{\zeta} h_{\zeta}^{\otimes n}) \end{array} \) (33)

with \(G_{[1]}^{[n]}\) defined in (80). Here we used the fact that \(\delta^n (h_{\zeta}^{\otimes n}) = \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^n_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\) (see Remark 48) and that by the definition of \(\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta}\) we obtain \(\forall \zeta \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\):

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} (\Phi^{[m + k]}_{\zeta}) = \varepsilon^{(m + k - 3) / 2} (m + k) ! \tilde{f}_{m + k, \varepsilon}\)

with \(\tilde{f}_{n, \varepsilon}\)\(\) the coefficients in the decomposition \(\tilde{F}_{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon}) := \sum_{n \geqslant 0} \tilde{f}_{n, \varepsilon} H_n (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon}, \sigma_{\varepsilon}^2)\). Choosing \(n = 4 - m\) in eq. (33) we obtain

\(\displaystyle \Phi_{\zeta}^{[m]} = \frac{3!}{(3 - m) !} f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - m}_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta}^{[m]} \) (34)

and a remainder with an infinite chaos decomposition strictly greater that \(3 - m\):

\(\displaystyle \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta} = \delta^{4 - m} (G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4 - m}) . \) (35)

This is a key step in the proof of Theorem 8. Indeed, it suffices to substitute (34) into definition (12) to identify the remainder \(\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\) in decomposition (28) that has to converge to zero, and see that it always contains the term \(\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\). Moreover, the structure (35) of \(\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta}\) makes it possible to bound its \(L^p\) norm and obtain (30), (31) in the same way as done in Section 3.1 for \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\). We will consider separately simple trees (namely \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}, \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}}\)) which are linear functions of \(\Phi^{[m]}\) in Section 3.3, and composite trees (namely \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}, \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}}\)) which are quadratic in simple trees and need to be further renormalized in order to converge to some limit as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\). We will show the decomposition (28) for composite trees in Section 3.4.1 and the bounds (30), (31) in Section 3.4.2.

Remark 9. We can easily estimate terms of the form \(\varepsilon^{- (m - 3) / 2} \Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta}\)\(\) for \(3 \leqslant m \leqslant 9\) and every \(p \in [2, \infty)\). We have (as already observed for \(F^{(1)}_{\varepsilon}\)):

\(\displaystyle \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{m - 3}{2}} \Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta} \right\|^p_{L^p} = \| F^{(m)}_{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta}) \|^p_{L^p} = \int_{\mathbb{R}} | F^{(m)}_{\varepsilon} (x) |^p \gamma (\mathrm{d} x)\)

where \(\gamma (\mathrm{d} x)\) is the density of a centered Gaussian with variance \(\sigma^2_{\varepsilon}\). The integral is finite by Assumption 1: in particular we only need to assume that the first \(m\) derivatives of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) have exponential growth (actually, it is easy to see that one can require even weaker growth conditions).

3.3Analysis of simple trees

First of all note that the term \(\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) has no remainder, and then it can be shown to converge in law to \(\lambda^{(2)} Y^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) by usual techniques (see [4]). In this section we show the convergence of the trees \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}\), \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\), \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\), \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\). We obtain easily from (34):

\(\displaystyle \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\tau} (t, x)\) \(\displaystyle :=\) \(\displaystyle \frac{(3 - m) !}{3!} \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta}\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle =\) \(\displaystyle f_{3, \varepsilon} \int_{\zeta} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{(3 - m)}_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mu_{\zeta} + \frac{(3 - m) !}{3!} \int_{\zeta} \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta}\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle =\) \(\displaystyle f_{\tau} (\lambda_{\varepsilon}) \Delta_q K^{\tau} (Y_{\varepsilon}) (t, x) + \Delta_q \hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon} (t, x),\) (36)

with \(\zeta = (s, y)\) and either

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{llllll} \mu_{q, \zeta} & = & \delta (t - s) K_{q, x} (y) \mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d} y, & & \text{for} & \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing}, \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}, \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}},\\ \mu_{q, \zeta} & = & \mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d} y \int K_{q, x} (z) P_{t - s} (z - y) \mathrm{d} z, & \quad & \text{for} & \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}, \end{array} \) (37)

where \(K_{q, x} (y)\) is the kernel associated to the Littlewood-Paley block \(\Delta_q\) and \(P_t (x)\) is the heat kernel.

As said before, \(f_{3, \varepsilon} \int_{\zeta} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{(3 - m)}_{\varepsilon, \zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mu_{q, \zeta}\) converges in law in \(L^p\) for every \(2 \leqslant p < + \infty\) to \(\lambda_3 \int_{\zeta} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{(3 - m)}_{\zeta} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mu_{q, \zeta}\) since \(f_{3, \varepsilon} \rightarrow \lambda_3\) by Assumption 1. We can bound the remainder term \(\int_{\zeta} \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta}\) in \(L^p (\Omega)\) using Lemma 38 and the definition of the norm \(\| \cdot \|_{\mathbb{D}^{4 - m, p} (H^{\otimes 4 - m})}\) to obtain:

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \int_{\zeta} \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & = & \left\| \delta^{4 - m} G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \left\| G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{\mathbb{D}^{4 - m, p} (H^{\otimes 4 - m})}\\ & \lesssim & \sum_{k = 0}^{4 - m} \left\| \mathrm{D}^k G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes 4 - m + k})} .\\ & & \end{eqnarray*}

From Corollary 42 we know that \((j - L)^{- 1}\) and \(\mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- 1}\) are bounded in \(L^p\) for every \(p \in [2, \infty)\) and every \(j \geqslant 1\). Applying repeatedly these estimations we obtain:

\(\displaystyle \left\| \mathrm{D}^k G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes 4 - m + k})} \lesssim \left\| \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes 4 - m})} .\)

Now we can proceed to implement the idea we already described in Section 3.1, i.e. estimating out the term \(\left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)}\) (which is bounded by Remark 9 but with infinite chaos decomposition) and considering the finite-chaos term that is left. We do this by decomposing the \(L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes 4 - m})\) norm as norms on \(H^{\otimes 4 - m}\) and \(L^{p / 2} (\Omega)\) as follows:

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes 4 - m})} & \lesssim & \left\| \left\| \int_{\zeta} \Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]} h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4 - m} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|^2_{H^{\otimes 4 - m}} \right\|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)}^{1 / 2}\\ & \lesssim & \left\| \int_{\zeta} \Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]} \Phi_{\zeta'}^{[4]} \langle h_{\zeta}^{\otimes 4 - m}, h_{\zeta'}^{\otimes 4 - m} \rangle_{H^{\otimes 4 - m}} \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} \right\|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)}^{1 / 2}\\ & \lesssim & \left[ \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \| \Phi_{\zeta}^{[4]} \Phi_{\zeta'}^{[4]} \|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)} | \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle |^{4 - m} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{1 / 2} . \end{eqnarray*}

Finally, putting the estimations together and using Hölder's inequality, together with the bound \(\varepsilon | \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle | = \varepsilon | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta') | \lesssim 1\) of Lemma 31, we obtain for every \(\delta \in (0, 1]\):

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \int_{\zeta} \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \left[ \varepsilon \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta'} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} | \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle |^{4 - m} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ & \lesssim & \left[ \varepsilon^{\delta} \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta'} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} | \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle |^{3 - m + \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}} . \end{eqnarray*}

Now using Remark 9 (note that to bound \(\varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} \Phi^{[4]}\) we only need to control the first 4 derivatives of \(F_{\varepsilon}\)) and the fact that \(\langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle_H = C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta')\) we obtain as a final estimation

\(\displaystyle \left\| \int_{\zeta} \hat{\Phi}^{(m)}_{\zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} \left[ \int | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta') |^{3 - m + \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{1 / 2} . \) (38)

From the definition (37) of the measure \(\mu_{q, \zeta}\), the l.h.s of (38) can be estimated in a standard way using Lemma 33 to obtain for every \(x \in \mathbb{T}^3\), \(q > 0\):

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lllllll} \left\| \Delta_q \hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (t, x) \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} 2^{- \frac{1 - \delta}{2} q} & & \left\| \Delta_q \hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (t, x) \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} 2^{\frac{1 + \delta}{2} q},\\ \left\| \Delta_q \hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (t, x) \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} 2^{\frac{2 + \delta}{2} q} & & \| \Delta_q \hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing} (t, x) \|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} 2^{\frac{\delta}{2} q} . \end{array}\)

Time regularity of trees

We want to show (31). In order to do that, we compute in the same way as before:

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \int_{\zeta} (\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{t, x} - \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{s, x}) \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} & \lesssim & \left\| \delta^{4 - m} \int_{\zeta} G^{[4 - m]}_{[1]} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} h^{\otimes 4 - m}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x} h_{s, x}^{\otimes 4 - m}) \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \left\| \left\| \int_{\zeta} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) h_{s, x}^{\otimes 4 - m} \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|^2_{H^{\otimes 4 - m}} \right\|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)}^{1 / 2}\\ & & + \left\| \left\| \int_{\zeta} \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x} (h_{t, x}^{\otimes 4 - m} - h_{s, x}^{\otimes 4 - m}) \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|^2_{H^{\otimes 4 - m}} \right\|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)}^{1 / 2} . \end{eqnarray*}

We focus on the first term above to obtain that it is bounded by

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \left\| \int_{\zeta} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x'} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x'}) \langle h_{s, x}^{\otimes 4 - m}, h_{s, x'}^{\otimes 4 - m} \rangle_{H^{\otimes 4 - m}} \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} \right\|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)}^{1 / 2}\\ & \lesssim & \left[ \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \| (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x'} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x'}) \|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{4 - m} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{1 / 2}\\ & \lesssim & \left[ \varepsilon \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \| \varepsilon^{- 1} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x'} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x'}) \|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{4 - m} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ & \lesssim & \left[ \varepsilon^{\delta} \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} \| \varepsilon^{- 1} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x'} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x'}) \|_{L^{p / 2} (\Omega)} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{3 - m + \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}} . \end{eqnarray*}

Now note that

\begin{eqnarray*} \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) & = & F^{(4)} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x)) - F^{(4)} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x))\\ & = & \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} \int_0^1 F^{(5)} [\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x) + \tau \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} (Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) - Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x))] (Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) - Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x)), \end{eqnarray*}

and we can estimate \(\left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} (\Phi^{[4]}_{t, x} - \Phi^{[4]}_{s, x}) \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)}\) by hypercontractivity and using Lemma 34 as

\begin{eqnarray*} & \lesssim_p & \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \left\| \int_0^1 F^{(5)} [\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x) + \tau \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} (Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) - Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x))] \right\|_{L^{2 p} (\Omega)} \| Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) - Y_{\varepsilon} (s, x) \|_{L^2 (\Omega)}\\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{1 / 2} [C_{\varepsilon} (0, 0) - C_{\varepsilon} (t - s, 0)]^{1 / 2} \\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{- 2 \sigma} | t - s |^{\sigma} \end{eqnarray*}

for any \(\sigma \in [0, 1 / 2]\). The other term can be estimated more easily by

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \left[ \varepsilon^{\delta} \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{2 - m + \delta} | \langle h_{t, x} - h_{s, x}, h_{t, x'} - h_{s, x'} \rangle | | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{- 2 \kappa} | t - s |^{\sigma} \left[ \varepsilon^{\delta} \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{3 - m + \delta + 2 \sigma} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}}, \end{eqnarray*}

and finally obtain

\(\displaystyle \left\| \int_{\zeta} (\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{t, x} - \hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{s, x}) \mu_{q, \zeta} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta / 2 - 2 \kappa} | t - s |^{\sigma} \left[ \int_{\zeta, \zeta'} | \langle h_{s, x}, h_{s, x'} \rangle |^{3 - m + \delta + 2 \sigma} | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} | \right]^{\frac{1}{2}} .\)

Which yields estimation (31) by applying Lemma 33 as before. This concludes the treatment of simple trees. Notice that in this section we only needed \(F_{\varepsilon} \in C^5 (\mathbb{R})\) with the first 5 derivatives having exponential growth: indeed we need to take 4 derivatives to bound \(\varepsilon^{1 / 2} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}\) as of Remark 9, plus one more derivative for the time regularity of \(\varepsilon^{1 / 2} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta}\).

3.4Analysis of composite trees

In this section we show the decomposition (28) and the bound (30) for the trees \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}, \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}}\). The time regularity (31) of \(\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\) can be obtained with the same technique as in the previous section, assuming that we can control one more derivative of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) than what is needed to prove the boundedness of \(Y^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\) (thus we will need \(F_{\varepsilon} \in C^9 (\mathbb{R})\) with exponential growth, as discussed in Remark 14). Looking at the definitions in (12) it is clear that we can write the \(q\)-th Littlewood-Paley blocks of \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\), \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\), \(\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) and \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) \(\forall \varepsilon > 0\) as:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{6} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}),\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{9} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}),\\ \Delta_q \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} - \bar{d}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}),\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-7.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} {- d'_{\varepsilon}}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-8.pdf}}} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (\bar{\zeta}) - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-9.pdf}}} \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}), \end{array} \) (39)

for any time-space point \(\bar{\zeta} = (t, \bar{x})\) that we keep fixed throughout this section. In order to keep the notation shorter we defined

\(\displaystyle \bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} := \varepsilon^{- 1 / 2} f_{2, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}},\)

which can be thought of as a finite-chaos equivalent of \(\Phi^{[1]}_{\varepsilon}\) (modulo a constant \(f_{2, \varepsilon} / f_{3, \varepsilon}\)) in the same way as \(\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) is a finite-chaos equivalent of \(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\)\(\). The measure \(\mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\)on \((\mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3)^2\) is given by

\(\displaystyle \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} := [\int_{x, y} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2,\)

with \(\zeta_{i :=} (s_i, x_i)\) for \(i = 1, 2\), \(K\) being the kernel associated to the Littlewood-Paley decomposition and \(P\) being the heat kernel. The first step for decomposing (39) is to expand them using the partial chaos expansion (82) to obtain

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2} & = & \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] {+ \delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}),\\ \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2} & = & \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] {+ \delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}),\\ \Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi_{\zeta_2}^{[1]} & = & \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] + \delta [J_0 \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2})] + \delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2})\\ & = & \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] + Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] + Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_2) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] + \delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) . \end{array} \) (40)

Like the trees appearing in the \(\Phi^4_3\) model, we expect composite trees to require a further renormalisation, on top of the Wick ordering. We developed (40) to the smallest order that allows us to see the effect of renormalization.

3.4.1Renormalisation of composite trees

In this section we show how to renormalize (39) by estimating terms of the type \(\mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}]\)\(\) in expansion (40). This poses an additional difficulty, as in principle we would need to compute an infinite number of contractions between \(\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_1}\) and \(\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}\). However, we can again decompose \(\)\(\Phi^{[m]}\) as in (34), and then the product formula (85) ensures that we only need to control a finite number of contractions. This is another important step in the proof and will be carried out in Lemma 12. First we need some preparatory results:

Lemma 10. We have

\(\displaystyle \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = \int_{s, x} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (s, \bar{x} - x) G (t - s, x) .\)

and

\(\displaystyle \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_2) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = \int_x \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x} - x) H (t, x),\)

where we introduced the kernels:

\begin{eqnarray*} G (t - s, x) & := & \int_{x_1', x_2} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s} (x_1') \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(t - s, x_2)}],\\ H (t, x) & := & \int_{s, x_1, x_1'} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) P_{t - s} (x_1' - x_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[2]}_{(t - s, - x_1)}] . \end{eqnarray*}

Remark 11. Some caveat on the notation: although we use the same letter for the kernel \(G (\cdot, \cdot)\) and the Green operator \(G_{[m]}^{[n]}\), those two are not related in any possible way. It is always clear which one the notation refers to.

Proof. We have

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\\ & = & \int_{s_1, x_1, x_2, x, x_1'} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (x_1' - x_1) Y_{\varepsilon} (s_1, x_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(t - s_1, x_2 - x_1)}] \end{eqnarray*}

and by change of variables, exploiting the translation invariance of the problem we obtain:

\(\displaystyle = \int_{s_1, x_1, x} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x + x_1) Y (s_1, x_1) \int_{x_1', x_2} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (x_1') \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(t - s_1, x_2)}] .\)

Using the definition of \(K_q\) we have

\(\displaystyle = \int_{s_1, x} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (s_1, \bar{x} - x) \int_{x_1', x_2} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (x_1') \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(t - s_1, x_2)}] .\)

Finally we can write

\(\displaystyle \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = \int_{s_1, x} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (s_1, \bar{x} - x) G (t - s_1, x) .\)

Similar computations holds for the other term, indeed

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} Y_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_2) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\\ & = & \int_{s_1, x_1, x_2, x, x_1'} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (x_1' - x_1) Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x_2) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[2]}_{(t - s_1, x_2 - x_1)}]\\ & = & \int_{x_2} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x + x_2) Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x_2) \int_{s_{1,} x_1, x, x_1'} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) P_{t - s_1} (x_1' - x_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[2]}_{(t - s_1, - x_1)}]\\ & = & \int_x \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x} - x) \int_{s_{1,} x_1, x_1'} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) P_{t - s_1} (x_1' - x_1) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[2]}_{(t - s_1, - x_1)}]\\ & = & \int_x \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x} - x) H (t, x) \end{eqnarray*}
\(\Box\)

Substituting the lemma above and (40) in the expressions (39), we can write them as:

\begin{eqnarray*} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{9} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}) \left[ \frac{1}{9} \int_{s, x} G (t - s, x) - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} \right]\\ \Delta_q \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}) \left[ \frac{1}{3} \int_{s, x} \bar{G} (t - s, x) - \bar{d}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} \right]\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{6} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}) \left[ \frac{1}{6} \int_x H (t, x) - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} \right]\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-7.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \delta^2 G_1^2 \mathrm{D}^2 (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \Delta_q (1) (\bar{\zeta}) \left[ \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} - d_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-8.pdf}}} \right]\\ & & + \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (\bar{\zeta}) \left[ \frac{1}{3} \int_{s, x} G (t - s, x) + \frac{1}{3} \int_x H (t, x) {- d'_{\varepsilon}}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-9.pdf}}} \right]\\ & & + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-10.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-11.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) \end{eqnarray*}

with the additional definitions

\begin{eqnarray*} \bar{G} (t - s, x) & := & \int_{x_1, x_1'} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) P_{t - s_1} (x_1' - x_1) \mathbb{E} [\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(t - s, - x_1)}],\\ \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & := & \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] G (t - s , x),\\ \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & := & \int_x [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] H (t, x) . \end{eqnarray*}

Now we can characterise the local behaviour of \(\mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}]\) appearing in the integrals above. Decomposing separately \(\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_1}\) and \(\)\(\Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}\) as in (34) we obtain:

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] & = & \frac{3!^2}{(3 - m) ! (3 - n) !} (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - m}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - n}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}] + \frac{3!}{(3 - m) !} f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - m}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_2}^{[n]}]\\ & & + \frac{3!}{(3 - n) !} f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - n}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_1}^{[m]}] +\mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_1}^{[m]} \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_2}^{[n]}], \end{eqnarray*}

where \(\mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - m}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^{3 - n}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}] = (3 - m) ! \delta (3 - m, 3 - n) C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^{3 - n}\) and to bound all other terms we introduce the following result.

Lemma 12. Under Assumption 1 (in particular if \(F \in C^8 (\mathbb{R})\) with exponentially growing derivatives) we have, for every \(0 \leqslant m, n \leqslant 3\) and \(m \leqslant n\):

\(\displaystyle | \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] | \lesssim \sum_{i = 0}^{4 - n} \varepsilon^{1 + \frac{n - m}{2} + i} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{4 - m + i} \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{3 - \frac{m + n}{2} + \delta}, \qquad \forall \delta \in [0, 1] .\)

Moreover for every \(0 \leqslant m, n \leqslant 3\),

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lllll} | \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] | & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{m + n - 3}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^m & \text{if} & m \geqslant 4 - n,\\ \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] & = & 0 & \text{if} & m < 4 - n. \end{array}\)

Proof. Using formula (83) we decompose

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] & = & \mathbb{E} [\delta^{4 - m} (G_{[1]}^{[4 - m]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_1} h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes 4 - m}) \delta^{4 - n} (G_{[1]}^{[4 - n]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_2} h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes 4 - n})]\\ & = & \sum_{i = 0}^{4 - n} \binom{4 - m}{i} \binom{4 - n}{i} i!\mathbb{E} (G_{[5 - n - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[8 - n - i]} G_{[5 - m - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi^{[8 - m - i]}_{\zeta_2}) \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle^{8 - m - n - i} . \end{eqnarray*}
\(\displaystyle = \sum_{i = 0}^{4 - n} \binom{4 - m}{i} \binom{4 - n}{i} i!\mathbb{E} (G_{[5 - n - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[8 - n - i]} G_{[5 - m - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi^{[8 - m - i]}_{\zeta_2}) \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle^{8 - m - n - i}\)

We can bound the term

\(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{\frac{m + n}{2} + i - 5} \mathbb{E} (G_{[5 - n - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[8 - n - i]} G_{[5 - m - i]}^{[8 - m - n - i]} \Phi^{[8 - m - i]}_{\zeta_2}) \lesssim \left\| \varepsilon^{\frac{n + i - 5}{2}} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[8 - n - i]} \right\|_{L^2} \left\| \varepsilon^{\frac{m + i - 5}{2}} \Phi_{\zeta_2}^{[8 - m - i]} \right\|_{L^2}\)

knowing \(8 - n - i \vee 8 - m - i \leqslant 8\) derivatives of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) (see Remark 9) and using the bound \(\varepsilon | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2) | \lesssim 1\) of Lemma 31 with \(| \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle | = | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2) |\) we have:

\(\displaystyle | \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] | \lesssim \sum_{i = 0}^{4 - n} \varepsilon^{1 + \frac{n - m}{2} + i} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{4 - m + i} \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{3 - \frac{m + n}{2} + \delta} .\)

For the second bound we recall that \(\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} = \delta^m (h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m})\) (Remark 48) and compute

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} \left[ \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} {\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}}_{\zeta_2} \right] & = & \mathbb{E} [\delta^m (h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m}) \delta^{4 - n} (G_{[1]}^{[4 - n]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_2} h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes 4 - n})]\\ & = & \sum_{i = 0}^{m \wedge 4 - n} \binom{m}{i} \binom{4 - n}{i} i!\mathbb{E} (\langle \mathrm{D}^{4 - n - i} (h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m}), G_{[m + 1 - i]}^{[m + 4 - n - i]} \Phi^{[4 + m - i]}_{\zeta_2} h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes m + 4 - n - i} \rangle_{H^{\otimes m + 4 - n - i}}) . \end{eqnarray*}

Since \(\mathrm{D} h^{\otimes m}_{\zeta_1} = 0\) we obtain \(\mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] = 0\) if \(m < 4 - n\) and

\begin{eqnarray*} | \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^m_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta_2}] | & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\frac{m + n - 3}{2}} \mathbb{E} [\varepsilon^{- \frac{3 - m - n}{2}} G_{[m + n - 3]}^{[m]} \Phi^{[m + n]}_{\zeta_2}] | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^m \end{eqnarray*}

if \(m \geqslant 4 - n\), with

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} [\varepsilon^{- \frac{3 - m - n}{2}} G_{[m + n - 3]}^{[m]} \Phi^{[m + n]}_{\zeta_2}] \lesssim 1.\)
\(\Box\)

Using Lemma 12 we obtain

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] & = & 9\mathbb{E} [(f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1}) (f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2})] = 18 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 [C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)]^2 +\mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \end{eqnarray*}

and thus \(G (t - s, x) = 18 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{x_1', x_2} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s} (x_1') [C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)]^2 + \hat{G} (t - s, x)\) with the remainder term defined by

\(\displaystyle \hat{G} (t - s, x) := \int_{x_1', x_2} \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s} (x_1') \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_0 \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(t - s, x_2)}] .\)

We have the estimation

\(\displaystyle | \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] | \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^{2 + \delta} . \) (41)

Similarly

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathbb{E} [\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] & = & 3 \varepsilon^{- 1 / 2} f_{2, \varepsilon} \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} (f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2})] = 6 \varepsilon^{- 1 / 2} f_{2, \varepsilon} f_{3, \varepsilon} [C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)]^2, \end{eqnarray*}

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] | & = & | \mathbb{E} [(f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1}) (6 f_{3, \varepsilon} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} + \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_2})] | \lesssim | f_{3, \varepsilon} | | \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] | + | \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}] |\\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\delta} (| f_{3, \varepsilon} | + 1) C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^{2 + \delta}, \end{array} \) (42)

and

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} | \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] | & = & | \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]} (3 f_{3, \varepsilon} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon {,} \zeta_2} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}^{[1]})] | \lesssim | f_{3, \varepsilon} | \mathbb{E} [\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] +\mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}]\\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{1 / 2} (| f_{3, \varepsilon} | + 1) C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^3 . \end{array} \) (43)

We have by Lemma 35 that for all \(\delta \in (0, 1)\) \(\begin{array}{lll} | \hat{G} (t - s, x) | & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\delta} (| t - s |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- 5 - \delta} \end{array} .\) Using estimate (78) together with Lemma 29, we have that for all \(\delta \in (0, 1)\), \(\delta' \in (0, \delta)\) that \(| H (t, x) | \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta'} (| t - s |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- \delta}\). Furthermore, we have

\(\displaystyle \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} = 6 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}\)

with the remainder term \(\Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}\) given by

\(\displaystyle \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} = \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] \hat{G} (t - s , x) .\)

The term

\(\displaystyle 6 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2\)

can be shown to converge in law to

\(\displaystyle 6 (\lambda_3)^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) \mathbb{E} (Y (0, 0) Y (s, x)) ^2\)

in \(C^{\kappa}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}\) with the standard techniques used in the analysis of the \(\Phi^4_3\) model. On the other hand, for all \(\delta > 0\) sufficiently small we have the bounds

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} \right\|_{L^{\infty}} + \left\| \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} \right\|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & \varepsilon^{\delta} \| Y_{\varepsilon} \|_{C^{\kappa}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}} 2^{q (1 / 2 + 2 \kappa + 2 \delta)} \int_{s, x} (| x | + | t - s |^{1 / 2})^{\delta - 5}\\ & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\delta} \| Y_{\varepsilon} \|_{C^{\kappa}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}} 2^{q (1 / 2 + 2 \kappa + 2 \delta)}, \end{eqnarray*}

which shows that these remainders go to zero in \(\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa} \) as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\), since \(\| Y_{\varepsilon} \|_{C^{\kappa}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}}\) is bounded in \(L^p (\Omega)\). Moreover, it is easy to see that \(\| \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} - \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} \|_{L^p (\Omega)} \sim O_{L^{\infty}} (2^{q (1 / 2 + 2 \kappa + 2 \delta)})\). Note that

\(\displaystyle \int_{s, x} G (t - s, x) = \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[1]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(s, x)}] = 18 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s, x} P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 + \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_0 \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(s, x)}],\)
\(\displaystyle \int_x H (t, x) = \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[2]}_{(s, x)}] = \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{(s, x)}] .\)

Here we used the fact that

\(\displaystyle \int_x \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) = \int_x \sum_{i, j} K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) = \delta (x_1'),\)

since \(\int_x K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (0) = 0\), where \(| i - j | > 1\). This is readily seen in Fourier space taking into account the support properties of the Littlewood-Paley blocks. Now,

\(\displaystyle \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_0 \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{(s, x)}], \qquad \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{(s, x)}],\)

converge to finite constants due to the bounds (41) and (42) and by Lemma 32 \(\int_{s, x} P_s (x) C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)^2 \lesssim | \log \varepsilon | .\)

Finally, from (43) we have

\(\displaystyle \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = \int_{s, x} P_s (x) \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_0 \Phi^{[1]}_{(s, x)}] = O (\varepsilon^{- 1 / 2}) .\)

Indeed Lemma 32 again yields \(\varepsilon \int_{s, x} P_s (x) C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)^3 \lesssim 1\).

Thus \(\int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathbb{E} [\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}] \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\) gives a diverging constant which depends on all the \((f_{n, \varepsilon})_n\). Making the choice to define the renormalisation constants \(d^{\tau}\) as in eq. (7) we cancel exactly these contributions which are either \((F_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}\) dependent and/or diverging. In particular we verify that we can satisfy the constraint (13).

Finally, noting that \(\delta G_1 \mathrm{D} = (1 - J_0)\) and \(\delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 = (1 - J_0 - J_1)\) (as seen in (82)), we can write the trees of (39) as

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_0) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \hspace{1em}\\ & & + \hspace{1em} \frac{f_{3, \varepsilon}}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \hspace{1em} \frac{1}{9} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2},\\ \Delta_q \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} f_{2, \varepsilon} f_{3, \varepsilon} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_0) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \hspace{1em} \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2},\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} Y_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\\ & & + \hspace{1em} \frac{f_{3, \varepsilon}}{6} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (6 \hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2} + \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \frac{1}{6} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} {\delta G_1} \mathrm{D} (\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2},\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_1) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta})\\ & & + 6 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} +\\ & & + \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 (3 \hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \frac{1}{3} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 (\hat{\Phi}^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} . \end{array} \) (44)

Let us summarize our results so far. We have shown that \(\Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) + \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) \sim O_{L^{\infty}} (\varepsilon^{\delta} 2^{q (1 / 2 + 2 \kappa + 2 \delta)})\) in \(L^p (\Omega)\) and then these terms converge to \(0\) in the right topology as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\). As already mentioned, the convergence in law of

\begin{eqnarray*} (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_0) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} & \rightarrow & (\lambda_3)^2 \Delta_q Y^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta})\\ \varepsilon^{- \frac{1}{2}} f_{2, \varepsilon} f_{3, \varepsilon} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_0) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^2_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} & \rightarrow & \lambda_3 \lambda_2 \Delta_q Y^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta})\\ (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} Y_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} & \rightarrow & (\lambda_3)^2 \Delta_q Y^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta})\\ (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} (1 - J_1) (\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y^3_{\varepsilon, \zeta_1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta_2}^2 \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} & & \\ + 6 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 & \rightarrow & (\lambda_3)^2 \Delta_q Y^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) \end{eqnarray*}

is easy to establish with standard techniques (as done in [4],[18]) assuming the convergence of \(\lambda_{\varepsilon}\) as in (9) to \(\lambda\). Then, comparing (44) with the canonical trees in (20) we can identify the remainder terms \(\Delta_q \hat{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\tau}\) that still need to be bounded, that are precisely those in which \(\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta}\) appears. Estimating these terms is the content of next section.

3.4.2Estimation of renormalised composite trees

In this section we prove the bound (30) for composite trees. The difficulty we encounter here is that the remainder \(\hat{Y}^{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\) cannot be written as an iterated Skorohod integral as in Section 3.1, but instead as a product of iterated Skorohod integrals. We will then use the product formula (85) to write the remainder in the desired form. We can write (44) in a much shorter way as:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{9} \delta G_1 {\int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2}} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \hspace{1em},\\ \Delta_q \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \delta G_1 \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D} (\bar{\Phi}^{[1]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2},\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{6} \delta G_1 {\int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2}} \mathrm{D} (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[2]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2},\\ \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) & = & \frac{1}{3} \delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D}^2 (\Phi^{[0]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[1]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q R_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} (\bar{\zeta}) + \frac{1}{3} \Delta_q \hat{R}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}}\\ & & + 6 (f_{3, \varepsilon})^2 \int_{s , x} [\Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t + s, \bar{x} - x) - \Delta_q Y_{\varepsilon} (t, \bar{x})] P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2, \end{array}\)

just substituting again \(\delta G_1 \mathrm{D} = (1 - J_0)\) and \(\delta^2 G_{[1]}^{[2]} \mathrm{D}^2 = (1 - J_0 - J_1)\). In order to treat all trees at the same time, we can write the first terms in the r.h.s. above (modulo a constant that we discard) as:

\(\displaystyle \delta^r G^{[r]}_{[1]} {\int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2}} \mathrm{D}^r (\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \quad \text{with $r = 1, i + j = 2$ or $r = 2, i + j = 1$.}\)

First notice that by Lemma 38 we have

\(\displaystyle \left\| \delta^r G_{[1]}^{[r]} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D}^r (\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \sum_{\theta = 0}^r \left\| \mathrm{D}^{\theta} G_{[1]}^{[r]} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D}^r (\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|_{L^p (H^{\otimes r + \theta})}\)

and from the boundedness of the operator \(\mathrm{D}^{\theta} G_{[1]}^{[r]}\) given by Corollary 42 we obtain:

\(\displaystyle \left\| \delta^r G_{[1]}^{[r]} \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D}^r (\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega)} \lesssim \left\| \int_{\zeta_1, \zeta_2} \mathrm{D}^r (\Phi^{[i]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[j]}_{\zeta_2}) \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes r})} .\)

Computing the \(r\)-th derivative of the integrand we obtain

\(\displaystyle \Phi^{[4 - m]}_{\zeta_1} \Phi^{[4 - n]}_{\zeta_2} h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h^{\otimes \ell}_{\zeta_2} = \left[ \frac{3! f_{3, \varepsilon}}{(3 - m) !} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\zeta_1}^{m - 1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \widehat{\Phi }_{\zeta_1}^{[4 - m]} \right] \left[ \frac{3! f_{3, \varepsilon}}{(3 - n) !} \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} Y_{\zeta_2}^{n - 1} \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}} + \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_2}^{[4 - n]} \right] h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes \ell} \) (45)

for \(m + n = 5\) and \(0 \leqslant k + \ell \leqslant 2\). The constraints on \(m, n, k, \ell\) are related to the number of branches in the graphical notation of the trees: each tree has \(m + k - 1\) leaves with height 2 and \(n + \ell - 1\) leaves with height 1, as follows

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} & \leftrightarrow & m + k = 3, \quad n + \ell = 3\\ \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} & \leftrightarrow & m = 3, k = 0, n = 2, \ell = 1\\ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} & \leftrightarrow & m + k = 4, \quad n + \ell = 2\\ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} & \leftrightarrow & \hspace{1em} m + k = 4, \quad n + \ell = 3. \end{array} \) (46)

In (45), the terms which do not contain \(\)\(\hat{\Phi}^{[n]}_{\zeta}\) will generate finite contributions in the limit, as seen in Section 3.4.1 by writing the decomposition (44). We just consider the terms proportional to \(\hat{\Phi}^{[4 - m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[4 - n]}_{\zeta_2}\), because all the other similar terms featuring at least one remainder \(\widehat{\Phi }_{\zeta}^{[m]}\) can be estimated with exactly the same technique, and are easily shown to be vanishing in the appropriate topology.

We can use one of the key observations of this paper, the product formula (85), to rewrite products of Skorohod integrals in the form \(\delta^m (u) \delta^n (v)\) as a sum of iterated Skorohod integrals \(\delta^{\ell} (w) \), which are bounded in \(L^p\) by Lemma 38. We obtain

\begin{eqnarray*} \hat{\Phi}^{[4 - m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}^{[4 - n]}_{\zeta_2} & = & \delta^m (G_{[1]}^{[m]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_1} h^{\otimes m}_{\zeta_1}) \delta^n (G_{[1]}^{[n]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_2} h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes n})\\ & = & \sum_{(q, r, i) \in I} C_{_{q, r, i}} \delta^{m + n - q - r} (\langle \mathrm{D}^{r - i} G_{[1]}^{[m]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_1} h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m}, \mathrm{D}^{q - i} G_{[1]}^{[n]} \Phi^{[4]}_{\zeta_2} h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes q + r - i}}) \\ & = & \sum_{(q, r, i) \in I} C_{_{q, r, i}} {\varepsilon^{1 + \frac{r + q}{2} - i}} \delta^{m + n - q - r} (\langle \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m + r - i}, \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2) h^{\otimes n + q - i}_{\zeta_2} \rangle_{H^{\otimes q + r - i}}) \end{eqnarray*}

with \(I = \{ (q, r, i) \in \mathbb{N}^3 : 0 \leqslant q \leqslant m, 0 \leqslant r \leqslant n, 0 \leqslant i \leqslant q \wedge r \}\) and the notation shortcut:

\(\displaystyle \Theta_{[i]}^{[j]} (\zeta) := \varepsilon^{- \frac{i}{2}} G^{[j]}_{[i]} \Phi_{\zeta}^{[3 + i]} .\)

By Remark 39, for every \(n, m \geqslant 1\) and \(\Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^n\) we can write \(\delta^n (\Psi) h^{\otimes m} = \delta^n (\Psi \otimes h^{\otimes m})\), and therefore

\(\displaystyle \int \hat{\Phi}^{[4 - m]}_{\zeta_1} \hat{\Phi}_{\zeta_2}^{[4 - n]} h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h^{\otimes \ell}_{\zeta_2} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} =\)
\(\displaystyle \sum_I C_{_{q, r, i}} {\varepsilon^{\frac{2 + q + r - 2 i}{2}}} \delta^{m + n - q - r} \int \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2) h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m - q} \otimes h^{\otimes n - r}_{\zeta_2} \otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h^{\otimes \ell}_{\zeta_2} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\)

With the term to estimate in this form, we can proceed as in Section 3.1 to estimate separately the terms \(\Theta_{[i]}^{[j]} (\zeta) = \varepsilon^{- \frac{i}{2}} G^{[j]}_{[i]} \Phi_{\zeta}^{[3 + i]}\) in \(L^p (\Omega)\), which are bounded as discussed in Remark 9.

Lemma 13. Under Assumption 1 (in particular if \(F_{\varepsilon} \in C^8 (\mathbb{R})\) and the first 8 derivatives have exponential growth) we have the bound:

\(\displaystyle \left\| \delta^{m + n - q - r} \int \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2) h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m - q} \otimes h^{\otimes n - r}_{\zeta_2} \otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h^{\otimes \ell}_{\zeta_2} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|^2_{L^p (H^{\otimes k + \ell})}\)

\(\displaystyle \lesssim \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | .\)

Proof. Thanks to Lemma 38 the integral can be estimated with

\(\displaystyle \sum_{j = 0, h \leqslant j}^{m + n - q - r} \left\| \int \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2) h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m - q} \otimes h^{\otimes n - r}_{\zeta_2} \otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h_{\zeta_2}^{\otimes \ell} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \right\|^2_{L^p (V)},\)

with \(V = H^{\otimes m + k + n + \ell - q - r + j}\). We have that \(\| \cdot \|_{L^p (H^{\otimes k + \ell})}^2 = \| \| \cdot \|^2_{H^{\otimes k + \ell}} \|_{L^{p / 2}}^{1 / 2}\) and therefore we can bound each term in the sum above as

\begin{eqnarray*} & \lesssim & (\int \| \langle \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2), \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta'_1) \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta'_2) \rangle_{H^{\otimes j}} \|_{L^{p / 2}} \times\\ & & \times | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |)^{1 / 2} \end{eqnarray*}

Using Hölder's inequality we get the estimate

\begin{eqnarray*} & & \| \langle \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2), \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta'_1) \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta'_2) \rangle_{H^{\otimes j}} \|_{L^{p / 2}}\\ & \lesssim & \| \langle \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1), \mathrm{D}^h \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta'_1) \rangle_{H^{\otimes h}} \|_{L^p} \| \langle \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2), \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta'_2) \rangle_{H^{\otimes j - h}} \|_{L^p} \end{eqnarray*}

Now to bound \(\| \langle \mathrm{D}^h \Theta_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} (\zeta), \mathrm{D}^h \Theta_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} (\zeta') \rangle_{H^{\otimes h}} \|_{L^p}\) (with \(h \leqslant j \leqslant m + n - q - r\) and \(a = r - i\)) we can use the boundedness of the operator \(\mathrm{D}^h G_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]}\) for \(h \leqslant 2 m\) given by Corollary 42. Consider the two regions \(h \leqslant 2 m\) and \(h > 2 m\). In the first region we just use Corollary 42 to obtain:

\begin{eqnarray*} \| \langle \mathrm{D}^h \Theta_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} (\zeta), \mathrm{D}^h \Theta_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} (\zeta') \rangle_{H^{\otimes h}} \|_{L^p} & \lesssim & \left\| \mathrm{D}^h G_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi^{[4 + a]}_{\zeta_1} \right\|^2_{L^{4 p} (H^{\otimes h})} \left\| \mathrm{D}^h G_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi^{[4 + a]}_{\zeta'_1} \right\|^2_{L^{4 p} (H^{\otimes h})}\\ & \lesssim & \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi^{[4 + a]}_{\zeta_1} \right\|_{L^{4 p}}^2 \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi^{[4 + a]}_{\zeta'_1} \right\|_{L^{4 p}}^2 . \end{eqnarray*}

If \(h > 2 m\) we first use the bound \(\mathrm{D}^{2 m} G_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]}\) and then take the remaining \(h - 2 m\) derivatives on \(\varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi^{[4 + a]}_{\zeta}\):

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \mathrm{D}^h G_{[1 + a]}^{[m + a]} \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[4 + a]} \right\|_{L^{4 p} (H^{\otimes h})}^2 & \lesssim & \left\| \mathrm{D}^{h - 2 m} \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a}{2}} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[4 + a]} \right\|_{L^{4 p} (H^{\otimes h - 2 m})}^2\\ & \lesssim & \left\| \varepsilon^{- \frac{1 + a + h - 2 m}{2}} \Phi_{\zeta_1}^{[4 + a + h - 2 m]} \right\|_{L^{4 p} (H^{\otimes h - 2 m})} \end{eqnarray*}

From Remark 9 we see that this last term is bounded by a constant if \(F \in C^{4 + a + h - 2 m} (\mathbb{R})\) with the first \(4 + a + h - 2 m\) derivatives having an exponential growth, with \(a = r - i\) and \(h \leqslant m + n - q - r\).

Applying the same reasoning to \(\| \langle \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2), \mathrm{D}^{j - h} \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta'_2) \rangle_{H^{\otimes j - h}} \|_{L^p}\) we conclude that we need to control \(4 + n \vee 4 + m\) derivatives of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) in order to perform the estimates of this Lemma. From the constraints (46) we see that \(4 + n \vee 4 + m \leqslant 8\).\(\Box\)

From Lemma 13 we obtain \(\forall \delta \in [0, 1 / 2)\): \(\varepsilon^{\frac{2 + q + r - 2 i}{2}}\)

\(\displaystyle \left\| \varepsilon^{\frac{2 + q + r - 2 i}{2}} \delta^{m + n - q - r} \int \Theta^{[m + r - i]}_{[1 + r - i]} (\zeta_1) \Theta_{[1 + q - i]}^{[n + q - i]} (\zeta_2) h_{\zeta_1}^{\otimes m - q} \otimes h^{\otimes n - r}_{\zeta_2} \otimes h^{\otimes k}_{\zeta_1} \otimes h^{\otimes \ell}_{\zeta_2} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \mu_{_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}} \right\|_{L^p (H^{\otimes k + \ell})}\)
\(\displaystyle \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta} \left( \varepsilon^{2 + q + r - 2 i - \delta} \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \left| \mu_{_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}} \right| \left| \mu_{_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'}} \right| \right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\)
\(\displaystyle := \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} (\mathfrak{I})^{\frac{1}{2}} .\)

Our aim now is to estimate the quantity \(\mathfrak{I}\). The idea is to use the bound \(\varepsilon | \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle | = \varepsilon C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta') \lesssim 1\) of Lemma 31 to cancel strategically some of the covariances \(| \langle h_{\zeta}, h_{\zeta'} \rangle |\). We will consider three regions:

If \(q + r \leqslant 2\) we use the bounds

\(\displaystyle {\varepsilon^{q + r - 2 i}} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \lesssim \varepsilon^2 | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^q | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^r\)

and then (we suppose \(r < 2\))

\(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{2 - r - \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} \lesssim | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - 2 + \delta}\)

to obtain

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I}\) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{r - \delta} \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - 2} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^q | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^r | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - 2 + \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^q | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | .\) (47)

(If vice-versa \(q < 2\) it suffices to put \(\delta\) on the term \(| \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + \delta}\).) Notice that in this case \(m + k - q > 0.\)

In the case \(q + r = 3\) if \(m + k - q \geqslant 2\) we estimate like before to obtain

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I}\) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{2 - \delta} \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{\frac{q + r}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{\frac{q + r}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{1 + \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | .\) (48)

Note that \(m + k - q + \delta - 1 > 0\) and \(m + k - q + 2 \delta - 3 > - 1\) here. If \(m + k - q = 1\) we bound

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathfrak{I} & \lesssim & \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r - 2} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{\frac{3 + \delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{\frac{3 + \delta}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | \end{eqnarray*} (49)

and note that \(m + k - q - 1 / 2 + \delta / 2 > 0\), \(m + k - q - 1 + \delta > 0\), \(n + \ell - r - 2 \geqslant 0\). Finally if \(m + k - q = 0\) we can only have \(m + k = 3, q = 3, r = 0, i = 0\) and thus

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I}\) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{3 - 2 \delta} \int | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\)
\(\displaystyle \) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \int | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell + m + k - 6} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \delta} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\) (50)

If \(\)\(q + r \geqslant 4\) we bound first

\(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{2 q + 2 r - 2 i + \delta - 4} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{q + r - i} \lesssim | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}}\)

(note that \(2 q + 2 r - 2 i + \delta - 4 \geqslant \delta\)) to obtain:

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I} \lesssim \varepsilon^{6 - q - r - \delta} \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\)

Now in the cases \(m + k = 3, n + \ell = 3\) and \(m + k = 4, n + \ell = 2\) we can just write \(\varepsilon^{6 - q - r - \delta} = \varepsilon^{m + k - q} \varepsilon^{6 - m - k - r - \delta}\) and cancel the corresponding number of covariances to obtain

\begin{eqnarray*} \mathfrak{I} & \lesssim & \int | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{\delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | \end{eqnarray*} (51)

while for the case \(m + k = 4, n + \ell = 3\) we have either \(\ell \geqslant 1\) or \(k \geqslant 1\) and therefore with one of the following bounds

\begin{eqnarray*} \varepsilon^{m + k - 1 - q} \varepsilon^{n + \ell - r - \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} & \lesssim & | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle | | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{\delta}\\ \varepsilon^{m + k - q} \varepsilon^{n + \ell - 1 - r - \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle |^{m + k - q} | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{n + \ell - r} & \lesssim & | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{1 + \delta} \end{eqnarray*}

we obtain the estimates

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I}\) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \int | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{1 + \delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |\) (52)
\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I}\) \(\displaystyle \lesssim\) \(\displaystyle \int | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_1'} \rangle | | \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^{\delta} | \langle h_{\zeta_1}, h_{\zeta_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \langle h_{\zeta'_1}, h_{\zeta'_2} \rangle |^{2 - \frac{\delta}{2}} | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} | .\) (53)

We can use directly Lemma 36 to obtain a final estimate of (47), (48), (49), (52). For \(\textrm{(\hyperref[e:graph-est-4]{50})}, \textrm{(\hyperref[e:graph-est-5]{51})}\) and (53) notice that the integral over \(\zeta_1, \zeta'_1\) is finite and thus the whole quantity is proportional to \(| \langle h_{\zeta_2}, h_{\zeta_2'} \rangle |^n\). Globally, we have

\(\displaystyle \mathfrak{I} \lesssim 2^{(m + k + n + \ell - 6) q}\)

as needed to prove (30).

Remark 14. Finally, by controlling one more derivative of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) as done in Section 3.3, we can show (31) for \(Y^{\tau} = Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}, \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}}\), thus proving that \(\hat{Y}^{\tau} \rightarrow 0\) in \(C^{\kappa / 2}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - \kappa}\) in probability \(\forall \alpha < | \tau |\). From the proof of Lemma 13 together with this observation, we conclude that we need to control the derivatives of \(F_{\varepsilon}\) up to order \(9\) to be able to show the convergence for composite trees.

4Convergence of the remainder and a-priori bounds

In this section we prove the convergence of the remainder (Lemma 19), as well as some technical results on the norm of the solution, needed in the proof of Theorem 7. In order to prove Lemma 19 we need first to prove Lemma 15, Lemma 16 and Lemma 17 in this order.

4.1Boundedness of the remainder

We show that the remainder \(R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon})\) that appears in equation (16) can be controlled by a stochastic term \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta}\) that converges to zero in probability, times a function of the solution \(v_{\varepsilon}\). Let.

\(\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) := \varepsilon^{\delta / 2} \|e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | Y_{\varepsilon} | + c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | P_{\cdot} (u_{0, \varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon} (0)) |} \|_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} . \) (54)

for \(p \in [1, \infty)\), \(\delta \in [0, 1]\) and define

\(\displaystyle v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} := v_{\varepsilon} - v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} \quad \text{with} \quad v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} : t \mapsto P_t (u_{0, \varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon} (0)) . \) (55)

Lemma 15. (Boundedness of remainder) For every \(\gamma \in (0, 1)\), \(\delta \in [0, 1]\) we have

\(\displaystyle \|R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}, v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) (t, x) \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma / (3 + \delta), p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \lesssim M_{\varepsilon} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) \| v_{\varepsilon} \|^{3 + \delta}_{\mathcal{M}_T^{\gamma / (3 + \delta)} L^{\infty} (\mathbb{T}^3)} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_T L^{\infty}}}\)

with \(M_{\varepsilon}\) as in (54), \(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\) as in (55) and \(\mathcal{M}^{\gamma / (3 + \delta), p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)\), \(\mathcal{M}_T^{\gamma / (3 + \delta)} L^{\infty} (\mathbb{T}^3)\) defined in (60).

Proof. We can write the remainder in two ways:

\begin{eqnarray*} R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) & = & v_{\varepsilon}^3 \int_0^1 [F_{\varepsilon}^{(3)} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} + \tau \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} v_{\varepsilon}) - F_{\varepsilon}^{(3)} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon})] \frac{(1 - \tau)^2}{2!} \mathrm{d} \tau\\ & = & \varepsilon^{\tfrac{1}{2}} v_{\varepsilon}^4 \int_0^1 F_{\varepsilon}^{(4)} (\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} Y_{\varepsilon} + \tau \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} v_{\varepsilon}) \frac{(1 - \tau)^3}{3!} \mathrm{d} \tau . \end{eqnarray*}

From assumption (8) on \(F_{\varepsilon}\) we obtain by interpolation of these two expressions, \(\forall \delta \in [0, 1]\), \(\forall t \geqslant 0, x \in \mathbb{T}^3\),

\(\displaystyle | R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) (t, x) | \lesssim \varepsilon^{\delta / 2} | v_{\varepsilon} (t, x) |^{3 + \delta} e^{c \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} | Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | + c \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} | v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | + c | \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) |},\)

and we estimate, \(\forall \gamma \in [0, 1)\),

\(\displaystyle \| t \mapsto t^{\gamma} R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) (t, x) \|_{L^p ((0, T], L^p (\mathbb{T}^3))} \lesssim \|t^{\frac{\gamma}{3 + \delta}} v_{\varepsilon} (t) \|_{C_T L^{\infty}}^{3 + \delta} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_T L^{\infty}}} \left\| \varepsilon^{\frac{\delta}{2}} e^{c \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} | Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | + c \varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}} | v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) |} \right\|_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} .\)
\(\Box\)

We can also verify that \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \rightarrow 0\) in probability for every \(\delta > 0\):

Lemma 16. (Convergence of the stochastic term) Under Assumption 1 the random variable \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon})\) defined in (54) converges to zero in probability \(\forall \delta \in (0, 1]\).

Proof. We can use Young's inequality to estimate \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon})\) for some \(c' > 0\) as

\begin{eqnarray*} M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) & \lesssim & \varepsilon^{\delta / 2} \|e^{c' \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | Y_{\varepsilon} |} \|_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} + \varepsilon^{\delta / 2} \|e^{c' \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | P_{.} Y_{\varepsilon} (0) |} \|_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)}\\ & & + \varepsilon^{\delta / 2} T^{1 / p} e^{c' \| \varepsilon^{1 / 2} u_{0, \varepsilon} \|_{L^{\infty}}} . \end{eqnarray*}

Under Assumptions 1 the term \(\| \varepsilon^{1 / 2} u_{0, \varepsilon} \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathbb{T}^3)}\) is uniformly bounded, so the third term above converges to zero in probability. Note that \(\varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x)\) and \(P_t \varepsilon^{1 / 2} Y_{\varepsilon} (t = 0)\) are centered Gaussian random variables, and then both \(\mathbb{E} \|e^{c' \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | Y_{\varepsilon} |} \|^p_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)}\) and \(\mathbb{E} \|e^{c' \varepsilon^{1 / 2} | P_{.} Y_{\varepsilon} (0) |} \|^p_{L^p [0, T] L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)}\) are uniformly bounded in \(\varepsilon > 0\) for every \(p \in [1, \infty)\). This yields the convergence in probability of \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon})\).\(\Box\)

In order to show that \(\)\(\| R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma', p}_T L^p} \rightarrow 0\) in probability for \(\gamma' > \frac{1}{4} + \frac{3}{2} \kappa\) as needed in the proof of Theorem 7, we still need to control the norms \(\| v_{\varepsilon} \|^{3 + \delta}_{\mathcal{M}_T^{\gamma / (3 + \delta)} L^{\infty}}\) and \(\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_T L^{\infty}}\) that appear in Lemma 15. This is done in next section.

4.2Apriori bounds on the solution

Lemma 17. (Apriori bound on the solution)

Fix \(T > 0\). There exists \(\kappa > 0\), \(T_{\star} = T_{\star} \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}, \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}}, | \lambda_{\varepsilon} | \right) \in (0, T]\) a lower semicontinuous function depending only on \(\left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}, \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}}, | \lambda_{\varepsilon} | \right)\) and a collection of events \((\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon > 0}\) such that

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{P} (\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}) \rightarrow 1 \qquad \text{as $\varepsilon \rightarrow 0$}\)

and conditionally on \(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}\) there exists a universal constant \(C > 0\) such that:

\(\displaystyle \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_{T_{\star}} \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \frac{1}{2} - \kappa}} + \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4} + \frac{3 \kappa}{2}} L^{\infty}} \leqslant C (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^3\)

for any \(v_{\varepsilon}\) that solves equation (16). Moreover, still conditionally on \(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{C_{T_{\star}} L^{\infty}} \leqslant C (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^3\)

with \(v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\) as in (55).

Proof. We know from Lemma 18 that the bounds above on \(\| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_{T_{\star}} \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \frac{1}{2} - \kappa}} + \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4} + \frac{3 \kappa}{2}} L^{\infty}}\) and \(\)\(\| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{C_{T_{\star}} L^{\infty}}\) hold whenever \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa / 2}\). The event \(\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon} = \{ M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa / 2} \}\) has \(\mathbb{P} (\mathcal{E}_{\varepsilon}) \rightarrow 1\) by Lemma 16 and this proves the result.\(\Box\)
