The only thing left to prove is Lemma 18, which just a standard application of some well-known bounds on paraproducts, that are recalled in Appendices A.2, and A.2.

First observe that for \(\varepsilon > 0\) a pair \((v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\natural}_{\varepsilon})\) solves the paracontrolled equation (16) if and only if \(v^{\natural}_{\varepsilon} = v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\) and \((v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon})\) solves:

\(\displaystyle \left\{ \begin{array}{lll} v_{\varepsilon} & = & - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} - \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} - 3 v_{\varepsilon} \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} + v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\\ \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} v_{\varepsilon}^{\flat} & = & U (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} ; v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) - R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon})\\ v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} (0) & = & Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} (0) + \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} (0) + 3 v_{\varepsilon, 0} \prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} (0) \end{array} \right. \) (56)

Here \(U\) is the same as in (16). The initial condition of (56) is given by \(v_{\varepsilon, 0} := u_{0, \varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon} (0)\). The a-priori bounds of Lemma 18 come from being able to find closed estimates for (56).

Let us specify now all the notations we are going to use in the rest of this section. We consider the spaces

\(\displaystyle \mathcal{V}_T^{\flat} := \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{2 \kappa}_T \cap \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 4, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}_T \cap \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2, 1 + 2 \kappa}_T, \quad \mathcal{V}_T := \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2, 1 / 2 - \kappa}_T \cap \mathscr{L} {\hspace{.2em}_T^{1 / 4 + 3 \kappa / 2, 2 \kappa}} ,\)

with the corresponding norms

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} & := & \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{2 \kappa}_T} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 4, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\flat} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2, 1 + 2 \kappa}_T}, \end{eqnarray*} (57)
\begin{eqnarray*} \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T} & := & \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2, 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 4 + 3 \kappa / 2, 2 \kappa}} . \end{eqnarray*} (58)

We refer to Appendix A.1 for the definition of the parabolic spaces \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T\). We let

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lllll} v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} & := & v_{\varepsilon} - v^{\sharp} & = & - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} - \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} - 3 (v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} + v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon},\\ v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash} & := & v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} & = & - \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} - 3 (v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} + v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}, \end{array}\)

and \(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} (t = 0) = v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash} (t = 0) = 0\). We define also the norm

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} & := & \| v^{\Box} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{2 \kappa}_T} + \| v^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{M}^{1 / 4}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}}, \end{eqnarray*}

with \(\mathcal{M}^{1 / 4}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}\) given in Appendix A.1. In order not to get lost in these definitions the reader can keep in mind the following:

  • \(v_{\varepsilon}\) is the solution without the linear term;

  • \(v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}\) is the contribution of the initial condition, which give origin to some explosive norm (near the initial time);

  • \(v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}\) is the regular part of the solution;

  • \(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}, v_{\varepsilon}^{\boxslash}\) enter in the estimation of the remainder, they are just convenient shortcuts for certain contributions appearing in \(v_{\varepsilon}\).

Lemma 18. There exists \(T_{\star} = T_{\star} \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}, \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}}, | \lambda_{\varepsilon} | \right) \in (0, T]\) a lower semicontinuous function depending only on \(\| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}\), \(\| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}}\) and \(| \lambda_{\varepsilon} |\), a constant \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} = M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) > 0\) defined by (54), and a universal constant \(C > 0\) such that, whenever \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa / 2}\) we have

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}^{\flat}} & \leqslant & C (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^3,\\ \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}} & \leqslant & C \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{T_{\star}}^{\flat}} \right) . \end{eqnarray*}

Proof. Using the well-known Schauder estimates of Lemma 21 (and the fact that \(\| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{\kappa, \alpha}} \lesssim T^{\kappa} \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{\alpha}}\)) we obtain for \(\kappa, \theta > 0\) small enough

\(\displaystyle \| I f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{- \theta + 2 \kappa, 2 \kappa}_T} + \| I f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 4 - \theta + 2 \kappa, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}_T} + \| I f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2 - \theta + 2 \kappa, 1 + 2 \kappa}_T} \lesssim T^{\frac{\kappa}{2}} \left( \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{1 - \theta}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \kappa}} + \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{1 / 2 + 2 \kappa} \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}} \right) . \) (59)

We choose \(\theta > 2 \kappa\) small enough so that

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{- \theta + 3 \kappa / 2, 2 \kappa}_T \cap \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 4 - \theta + 3 \kappa / 2, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}_T \cap \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{1 / 2 - \theta + 3 \kappa / 2, 1 + 2 \kappa}_T \subseteq \mathcal{V}_T^{\flat} .\)

Now

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} & \lesssim & \|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} + \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} + \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_{_T} L^{\infty}} (\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{1 - \kappa}} +\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 - \kappa}})\\ & & + \left( \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp} \|_{\mathcal{M}_T^{1 / 4} \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \kappa}} \right) (\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{1 - \kappa}} +\|Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 - \kappa}}) + \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\flat} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}}\\ & \lesssim & \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + T^{\kappa} \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} \end{eqnarray*}

where \(\)we used that \(v^{\Box} (0) = 0\) and as a consequence that \(\|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_{_T} L^{\infty}} \leqslant T^{\kappa} \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{C_{_T}^{\kappa} L^{\infty}} \leqslant T^{\kappa} \| v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} \) to gain a small power of \(T\). So provided \(T\) is small enough (depending only on \(\mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}\)) this yields the following a-priori estimation on \(v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box}\):

\(\displaystyle \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{C_T L^{\infty}} \lesssim \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} \lesssim \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} .\)

Therefore we have an estimation on \(v_{\varepsilon}\):

\(\displaystyle \| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T} \leqslant \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\sharp} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T} + \| v^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T} \lesssim \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}} \lesssim \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} .\)

In order to estimate terms in \(U (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} ; v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\) we decompose the renormalised products as

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} \hat{\diamond} v_{\varepsilon} & = & v_{\varepsilon} \succ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}} - \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}} - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} - 3 v_{\varepsilon} Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} + v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} \circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-7.pdf}}} - 3 \overline{\operatorname{com}}_1 (v_{\varepsilon}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-8.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-9.pdf}}})\\ v_{\varepsilon} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon} & = & - \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-10.pdf}}} Y_{\varepsilon} - 3 (v_{\varepsilon} \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-11.pdf}}}) Y_{\varepsilon} + Y_{\varepsilon} \preccurlyeq (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) + Y_{\varepsilon} \succ (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) \\ & & - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-12.pdf}}} \prec Y_{\varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-13.pdf}}} \succ Y_{\varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-14.pdf}}}\\ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-15.pdf}}} \diamond v_{\varepsilon}^2 & = & Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-16.pdf}}} \diamond (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-17.pdf}}})^2 + 2 (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-18.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-19.pdf}}}) (\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-20.pdf}}} + 3 v_{\varepsilon} \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-21.pdf}}}) - 2 (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-22.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-23.pdf}}}) \preccurlyeq (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\\ & & + 2 (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-24.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-25.pdf}}}) \succ (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-26.pdf}}} \preccurlyeq (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp})^2 + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-27.pdf}}} \succ (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp})^2 . \end{array}\)

We decompose \(U (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} ; v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})\) as

\begin{eqnarray*} U (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} ; v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) & = & Q_{- 1 / 2} (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}, v_{0, \varepsilon}, v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}) + Q_0 (\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}, v_{0, \varepsilon}, v_{\varepsilon}, v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon}) + Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}\\ Q_{- 1 / 2} & := & - 3 [v_{\varepsilon} \succ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-1.pdf}}} - 3 \overline{\operatorname{com}}_1 (v_{\varepsilon}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-2.pdf}}}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-3.pdf}}}) + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-4.pdf}}} \succ (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp})^2]\\ & & - 6 [(Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-5.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-6.pdf}}}) (3 v_{\varepsilon} \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-7.pdf}}}) + (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-8.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-9.pdf}}}) \succ (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})]\\ & & + 2 \lambda_{2, \varepsilon} (3 (v_{\varepsilon} \prec\!\!\!\prec Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-10.pdf}}}) Y_{\varepsilon} - Y_{\varepsilon} \succ (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) ) + 3\operatorname{com}_3 (v_{\varepsilon}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-11.pdf}}}) + 3\operatorname{com}_2 (v_{\varepsilon}, Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-12.pdf}}})\\ Q_0 & := & 3 [3 v_{\varepsilon} Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-13.pdf}}} - v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-14.pdf}}} - v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon} \circ Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-15.pdf}}} + 2 (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-16.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-17.pdf}}}) \preccurlyeq (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon}) - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-18.pdf}}} \preccurlyeq (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp})^2]\\ & & - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\varnothing} v_{\varepsilon}^3 - \lambda_{2, \varepsilon} [v_{\varepsilon}^2 + 2 Y_{\varepsilon} \preccurlyeq (v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} + v^{\sharp}_{\varepsilon})]\\ Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}} & := & (1 - \lambda_{1, \varepsilon}) Y_{\varepsilon} - \lambda_{0, \varepsilon} + 3 [\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-19.pdf}}} + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-20.pdf}}} - Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-21.pdf}}} \diamond (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-22.pdf}}})^2 - 2 (Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{0.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-23.pdf}}} \diamond Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-24.pdf}}}) \bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-25.pdf}}}]\\ & & + 2 \lambda_{2, \varepsilon} (\bar{Y}_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-26.pdf}}} Y_{\varepsilon} + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-27.pdf}}} \prec Y_{\varepsilon} + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-28.pdf}}} \succ Y_{\varepsilon} + Y_{\varepsilon}^{\resizebox{1.5em}{!}{\includegraphics{image-29.pdf}}}) . \end{eqnarray*}

Here \(Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}\) does not depend from the solution but only on \(\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}\) (as the notation suggests) and we have grouped the other terms which we expect to have regularity \(\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}\) in \(Q_{- 1 / 2}\), (and the same for \(Q_0\) and regularity \(\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- k}\)). With the same technique we used above for \(v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon}\), we obtain the following estimate on \(v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon}\)

\(\displaystyle \| v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{1 / 2 + 3 \kappa / 2, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}} + \| v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{1 / 4 + \kappa, \kappa}} \lesssim \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}\)

and this yields

\(\displaystyle \| (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon})^2 \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{3 / 4 + 5 \kappa / 2, 1 / 2 + 2 \kappa}} + \| (v^{\boxslash}_{\varepsilon})^2 \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{1 / 2 + 2 \kappa, \kappa}} \lesssim \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T} \right)^2 .\)

Then we are ready to bound \(Q_{- 1 / 2}, Q_0, Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}}\) using the standard paraproducts estimations recalled in Appendix A.2:

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| Q_{- 1 / 2} \|_{\mathcal{M}_T^{1 / 2 + 2 \kappa} \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}} + \| Q_0 \|_{\mathcal{M}^{1 - \theta}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \kappa}} & \lesssim & (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} + \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T} \right)^3\\ \| Q_{\lambda_{\varepsilon}, \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon}} \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} & \lesssim & (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 . \end{array}\)

In order to conclude the estimation of \(\| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}\) we have to control \(\| I R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}\). This is achieved easily by the using the results of Section 4.1. Thanks to Lemma 15 \(\forall \delta \in (0, 1)\), \(\forall \theta > 0\) such that \(\frac{1 - \theta}{3 + \delta} > \frac{1}{4} + \frac{3 \kappa}{2}\) (note that it is possible to choose \(\theta > 2 \kappa\) that satisfies this property as long as \(k\) and \(\delta\) are small enough) we have:

\(\displaystyle \|R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) \|_{\mathcal{M}^{1 - \theta, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \lesssim M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) \| v_{\varepsilon} \|^{3 + \delta}_{\mathcal{V}_T} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\Box}_T}} .\)

\(\)By Lemma 22 together with (61) we obtain then

\begin{eqnarray*} \| I R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T} & \lesssim & M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) \| v_{\varepsilon} \|^{3 + \delta}_{\mathcal{V}_T} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \|v^{\Box}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\Box}}} . \end{eqnarray*}

Using that

\(\displaystyle \| P_{\cdot} v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} (0) \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} \lesssim \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} (0) \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{1 / 2 - 2 \kappa}} \lesssim \left( 1 + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right) \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}\)

we obtain that \(\exists C' > 0\) such that

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}} & \leqslant & C' (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon_n} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon_n} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^3 + C' T^{\kappa / 2} (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}^3\\ & & + C' M_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}}} \| v_{\varepsilon} \|^{3 + \delta}_{\mathcal{V}_T}\\ & \leqslant & D + CM_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}}} + CT^{\kappa / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}^3 + CM_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_T^{\flat}}} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_T}^{3 + \delta} \end{eqnarray*}

with

\(\displaystyle C := C' [(1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 + e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)} \left( 1 + \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T} + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^{3 + \delta} \right)],\)

and

\(\displaystyle D := C' (1 + | \lambda_{\varepsilon_n} |) (1 + \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon_n} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T})^3 \left( 1 + \| v_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}} \right)^3 .\)

Let \(T_{\star} \in (0, T]\) such that:

\(\displaystyle CT_{\star}^{\kappa / 2} [(5 C)^2 + e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} (5 C)} (5 C)^{2 + \delta}] \leqslant \frac{1}{2} \text{, \quad and } CT_{\star}^{\kappa / 2} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} (5 C)} \leqslant D.\)

Assume that \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \leqslant T_{\star}^{\kappa / 2}\). Define a closed interval \([0, S] = \{t \in [0, T_{\star}] : \| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_t^{\flat}} \leqslant 4 D\} \subseteq [0, T_{\star}]\). This interval is\(\) well defined and non–empty since \(\)\(t \mapsto \| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_t^{\flat}}\) is continuous and nondecreasing and \(\| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_0^{\flat}} \leqslant 4 D\)\(\). Let us assume that \(S < T_{\star}\), then we can take \(\epsilon > 0\) small enough such that \(S + \epsilon < T_{\star}\) and by continuity \(\)\(\| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{S + \epsilon}^{\flat}} \leqslant 5 C\), then

\begin{eqnarray*} \| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_{S + \epsilon}^{\flat}} & \leqslant & D + CM_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{S + \epsilon}^{\flat}}} + C (S + \epsilon)^{\kappa / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S + \epsilon}}^3 + CM_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}_{S + \epsilon}^{\flat}}} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S + \epsilon}}^{3 + \delta}\\ & \leqslant & D + CM_{\varepsilon, \delta} (Y_{\varepsilon}, u_{0, \varepsilon}) e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} (5 C)} + CT_{\star}^{\kappa / 2} (5 C)^2 \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S + \epsilon}} + CT_{\star}^{\kappa / 2} e^{c \varepsilon^{1 / 2} (5 C)} (5 C)^{2 + \delta} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S + \epsilon}}\\ & \leqslant & 2 D + \frac{1}{2} \| v^{\flat}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{V}^{\flat}_{S + \epsilon}} \end{eqnarray*}

which gives \(\| v^{\flat}_{{\varepsilon_n} } \|_{\mathcal{V}_{S + \epsilon}^{\flat}} \leqslant 4 D\). This implies \(S = T_{\star}\) (by contradiction). From the construction of \(T_{\star}\) it is easy to see that \(T_{\star} \left( \| \mathbb{Y}_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{X}_T}, \| u_{\varepsilon, 0} \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1 / 2 - \kappa}}, | \lambda_{\varepsilon} | \right)\) is lower semicontinuous.\(\Box\)

4.3Convergence of the remainder

It suffices to put together the results obtained in Sections 4.1 and 4.2 to obtain the convergence of \(R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon})\):

Lemma 19. The remainder \(R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon})\) that appears in equation (16) converges in probability to \(0\) as \(\varepsilon \rightarrow 0\) in the space \(\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_{T_{\star}} L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)\).

Proof. From the estimation on \(R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon})\) of Lemma 15, together with the fact that \(M_{\varepsilon, \delta} \rightarrow 0\) in probability (Lemma 16) and the bounds on \(\)\(\| v_{\varepsilon} \|_{\mathcal{M}_{T_{\star}}^{\frac{1}{4} + \frac{3 \kappa}{2}} L^{\infty}}\) and \(\| v_{\varepsilon}^{\Box} \|_{C_T L^{\infty}}\) of Lemma 17 we see immediately that \(\)\(\| R_{\varepsilon} (v_{\varepsilon}) \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_{T_{\star}} L^p} \rightarrow 0\) in probability.\(\Box\)

Appendix AParacontrolled analysis and kernel estimations

In this section we first recall the the basic results of paracontrolled calculus first introduced in [6], without proofs. For more details on Besov spaces, Littlewood–Paley theory, and Bony's paraproduct the reader can refer to the monograph [2]. We then proceed to give some results on the convolution of functions with known singularity and the estimation of finite-chaos Gaussian trees. We refer to Section 10 of [8] and to the nice pedagogic exposition [18] for further details.

A.1Notation

Throughout the paper, we use the notation \(a \lesssim b\) if there exists a constant \(c > 0\), independent of the variables under consideration, such that \(a \leqslant c \cdot b\). If we want to emphasize the dependence of \(c\) on the variable \(x\), then we write \(a (x) \lesssim_x b (x)\). If \(f\) is a map from \(A \subset \mathbb{R}\) to the linear space \(Y\), then we write \(f_{s, t} = f (t) - f (s)\). For \(f \in L^p (\mathbb{T}^d)\) we write \(\|f (x)\|^p_{L^p_x (\mathbb{T}^3)} := \int_{\mathbb{T}^3} |f (x) |^p \mathrm{d} x\).

Given a Banach space \(X\) with norm \(\| \cdot \|_X\) and \(T > 0\), we note \(C_T X = C ([0, T], X)\) for the space of continuous maps from \([0, T]\) to \(X\), equipped with the supremum norm \(\lVert \cdot \rVert_{C_T X}\), and we set \(C X = C (\mathbb{R}_+, X)\). For \(\alpha \in (0, 1)\) we also define \(C^{\alpha}_T X\) as the space of \(\alpha\)-Hölder continuous functions from \([0, T]\) to \(X\), endowed with the seminorm \(\|f\|_{C^{\alpha}_T X} = \sup_{0 \leqslant s < t \leqslant T} \|f (t) - f (s)\|_X / |t - s|^{\alpha}\), and we write \(C^{\alpha}_{\operatorname{loc}} X\) for the space of locally \(\alpha\)-Hölder continuous functions from \(\mathbb{R}_+\) to \(X\). For \(\gamma > 0\), \(p \in [1, \infty)\), we define

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T X & = & \{ v : L^p ((0, T], X) : \| v \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T X} = \| t \mapsto t^{\gamma} v (t) \|_{L^p ((0, T], X)} < \infty \},\\ \mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_T X & = & \{ v : C ((0, T], X) : \| v \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_T X} = \| t \mapsto t^{\gamma} v (t) \|_{C_T X} < \infty \} . \end{array} \) (60)

The space of distributions on the torus is denoted by \(\mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}' (\mathbb{T}^3)\) or \(\mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}'\). The Fourier transform is defined with the normalization

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em} u (k) = \hat{u} (k) = \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} e^{- \iota \langle k, x \rangle} u (x) \mathrm{d} x, \qquad k \in \mathbb{Z}^3,\)

so that the inverse Fourier transform is given by \(\mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1} v (x) = (2 \pi)^{- 1} \sum_k e^{\iota \langle k, x \rangle} v (k)\). Let \((\chi, \rho)\) denote a dyadic partition of unity such that \(\operatorname{supp} (\rho (2^{- i} \cdot)) \cap \operatorname{supp} (\rho (2^{- j} \cdot)) = \emptyset\) for \(|i - j| > 1\). The family of operators \((\Delta_j)_{j \ge - 1}\) will denote the Littlewood-Paley projections associated to this partition of unity, that is \(\Delta_{- 1} u = \mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1} \left( \chi \mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em} u \right)\) and \(\Delta_j = \mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em}^{- 1} \left( \rho (2^{- j} \cdot) \mathscr{F} \hspace{.1em} u \right)\) for \(j \ge 0\). Let \(S_j = \sum_{i < j} \Delta_i\), and \(K_q\)be the kernel of \(\Delta_q\) so that

\(\displaystyle \Delta_q f (\bar{x}) = \int_{\mathbb{T}^3} K_{\bar{x}, q} (x) f (x) \mathrm{d} x.\)

For the precise definition and properties of the Littlewood-Paley decomposition \(f = \sum_{q \geqslant - 1} \Delta_q f\) in \(\)\(\mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}' (\mathbb{T}^3)\), see Chapter 2 of [2]. The Hölder-Besov space \(B^{\alpha}_{p, q} (\mathbb{T}^3, \mathbb{R})\) for \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\) ,\(p, q \in [1, \infty]\) with \(B^{\alpha}_{p, q} (\mathbb{T}^3, \mathbb{R}) = : \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^a\) is and equipped with the norm

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \lVert f \rVert_{\alpha} = \lVert f \rVert_{B^{\alpha}_{\infty, \infty}} & = & \sup_{i \geqslant - 1} (2^{i \alpha} \| \Delta_i f \|_{L^{\infty} (\mathbb{T}^3)}),\\ \lVert f \rVert_{B^{\alpha}_{p, q}} & = & \| 2^{i \alpha} \| \Delta_i f \|_{L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \|_{\ell^q} . \end{array}\)

If \(f\) is in \(\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - \varepsilon}\) for all \(\varepsilon > 0\), then we write \(f \in \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha -}\). For \(\alpha \in (0, 2)\), we define the space \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{\alpha} = C^{\alpha / 2}_T L^{\infty} \cap C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}\), equipped with the norm

\(\displaystyle \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{\alpha}} = \max \left\{ \| f \|_{C^{\alpha / 2}_T L^{\infty}}, \| f \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \right\} .\)

The notation is chosen to be reminiscent of

\(\displaystyle \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} := \partial_t - \Delta,\)

by which we will always denote the heat operator with periodic boundary conditions on \(\mathbb{T}^d\). We also write \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\alpha} = C^{\alpha / 2}_{\operatorname{loc}} L^{\infty} \cap C \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}\). When working with irregular initial conditions, we will need to consider explosive spaces of parabolic type. For \(\gamma \geqslant 0\), \(\alpha \in (0, 1)\), and \(T > 0\) we define the norm

\(\displaystyle \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T} = \max \left\{ \| t \mapsto t^{\gamma} f (t) \|_{C^{\alpha / 2}_T L^{\infty}}, \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \right\},\)

and the space \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T = \left\{ f : [0, T] \rightarrow \mathbb{R}: \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T} < \infty \right\}\). In particular, we have \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{0, \alpha}_T = \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\alpha}_T\). We introduce the linear operator \(I : C \left( \mathbb{R}_+, \mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}' (\mathbb{T}) \right) \rightarrow C \left( \mathbb{R}_+, \mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}' (\mathbb{T}) \right)\) given by

\(\displaystyle I f (t) = \int_0^t P_{t - s} f (s) \mathrm{d} s,\)

where \((P_t)_{t \geqslant 0}\) is the heat semigroup with kernel \(P_t (x) = \frac{1}{(4 \pi t)^{3 / 2}} e^{- \frac{| x |^2}{4 t}} \mathbb{I}_{t \geqslant 0}\).

Paraproducts are bilinear operations introduced by Bony [3] in order to linearize a class of non-linear PDE problems. They appear naturally in the analysis of the product of two Besov distributions. In terms of Littlewood–Paley blocks, the product \(fg\) of two distributions \(f\) and \(g\) can be formally decomposed as

\(\displaystyle fg = f \prec g + f \succ g + f \circ g,\)

where

\(\displaystyle f \prec g = g \succ f := \sum_{j \geqslant - 1} \sum_{i = - 1}^{j - 2} \Delta_i f \Delta_j g \quad \text{and} \quad f \circ g := \sum_{|i - j| \leqslant 1} \Delta_i f \Delta_j g.\)

This decomposition behaves nicely with respect to Littlewood–Paley theory. We call \(f \prec g\) and \(f \succ g\) paraproducts, and \(f \circ g\) the resonant term. We use the notation \(f \preccurlyeq g = f \prec g + f \circ g\). The basic result about these bilinear operations is given by the following estimates, essentially due to Bony [3] and Meyer [14].

When dealing with paraproducts in the context of parabolic equations it would be natural to introduce parabolic Besov spaces and related paraproducts. But to keep a simpler setting, we choose to work with space–time distributions belonging to the scale of spaces \(\left( C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha} \right)_{\alpha \in \mathbb{R}}\) for some \(T > 0\). To do so efficiently, we will use a modified paraproduct which introduces some smoothing in the time variable that is tuned to the parabolic scaling. Let therefore \(\varphi \in C^{\infty} (\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{R}_+)\) be nonnegative with compact support contained in \(\mathbb{R}_+\) and with total mass \(1\), and define for all \(i \geqslant - 1\) the operator

\(\displaystyle Q_i : C \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta} \rightarrow C \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}, \qquad Q_i f (t) = \int_0^{\infty} 2^{- 2 i} \varphi (2^{2 i} (t - s)) f (s) \mathrm{d} s.\)

We will often apply \(Q_i\) and other operators on \(C \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}\) to functions \(f \in C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}\) which we then simply extend from \([0, T]\) to \(\mathbb{R}_+\) by considering \(f (\cdot \wedge T)\). With the help of \(Q_i\), we define a modified paraproduct

\(\displaystyle f \prec\!\!\!\prec g := \sum_i (Q_i S_{i - 1} f) \Delta_i g\)

for \(f, g \in C \left( \mathbb{R}_+, \mathscr{D} \hspace{.1em}' (\mathbb{T}) \right)\). We define the commutators \(\operatorname{com}_1, \overline{\operatorname{com}}_1, \operatorname{com}_2, \operatorname{com}_3\) in Lemma 26. We write \(\check{P} (t, x) = \frac{1}{(4 \pi t)^{3 / 2}} e^{- \frac{| x |^2}{4 t}} e^{- t} \mathbb{1}_{t \geqslant 0}\) for a modified heat kernel which has the same bounds of the usual heat kernel \(P (t, x)\). Let \(Y_{\varepsilon}\) as in (3) and recall that \(C_{\varepsilon}\) is the covariance of \(Y_{\varepsilon}\) , i.e. \(C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) =\mathbb{E} (Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x) Y_{\varepsilon} (0, 0))\). We will sometimes write \(Y_{\varepsilon, \zeta} := Y_{\varepsilon} (t, x)\) for \(\zeta = (t, x) \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\). Let \(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^2 = \varepsilon \mathbb{E} [ (Y_{\varepsilon} (0, 0))^2] = \varepsilon C_{\varepsilon} (0, 0)\).

A.2Basic paracontrolled calculus results

First, let us recall some interpolation results on the parabolic time-weighted spaces \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T\):

Lemma 20. For all \(\alpha \in (0, 2)\), \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\), \(\varepsilon \in [0, \alpha \wedge 2 \gamma)\), \(T > 0\) and \(f \in \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T\) with \(f (0) = 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma - \varepsilon / 2, \alpha - \varepsilon}_T} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T} .\) (61)

Let \(\alpha \in (0, 2)\), \(\gamma \in (0, 1),\) \(T > 0\), and let \(f \in \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\alpha}_T\). Then for all \(\delta \in (0, \alpha]\) we have

\(\displaystyle \begin{array}{lll} \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\delta}_T} & \lesssim & \| f (0) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\delta}} + T^{(\alpha - \delta) / 2} \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\alpha}_T},\\ \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \delta}_T} & \lesssim & T^{(\alpha - \delta) / 2} \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T} . \end{array} \) (62)

Schauder estimates

Lemma 21. Let \(\alpha \in (0, 2)\) and \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\). Then

\(\displaystyle {\|I f\|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_t}} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_t \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - 2}},\) (63)

for all \(t > 0\). If further \(\beta \geqslant - \alpha\), then

\(\displaystyle \| s \mapsto P_s u_0 \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{(\beta + \alpha) / 2, \alpha}_t} \lesssim \| u_0 \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{- \beta}} .\) (64)

For all \(\alpha \in \mathbb{R}\), \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\), and \(t > 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle {\|I f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_t \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}}} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_t \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - 2}} .\) (65)

Proofs can be found e.g. in [7]. We need also some well known bounds for the solutions of the heat equation with sources in space–time Lebesgue spaces.

Lemma 22. Let \(\beta \in \mathbb{R}\) and \(f \in L^p_T B^{\beta}_{p, \infty}\), then for every \(\kappa \in [0, 1]\) we have \(I f \in C_T^{\kappa / q} \mathcal{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}}^{\beta + 2 (1 - \kappa) - (2 - 2 \kappa + d) / p}\) with

\(\displaystyle \|I f\|_{C_T^{\kappa / q} \mathcal{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}}^{\beta + 2 (1 - \kappa) - (2 - 2 \kappa + d) / p}} \lesssim_T \|f\|_{L^p_T B^{\beta}_{p, \infty}},\)

with \(\frac{1}{q} + \frac{1}{p} = 1\). Moreover, for every \(\gamma < \gamma' < 1 - 1 / p\) and every \(0 < \alpha < (2 - 5 / p + \beta) \wedge 2\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| I f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma', \alpha}_T} \lesssim_T \| f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T B^{\beta}_{p, \infty}} .\)

Proof. We only show the second inequality as the first one is easier and obtained with similar techniques. Let \(u = I f\), we have

\begin{eqnarray*} t^{\gamma} \| \Delta_i u (t)\|_{L^{\infty}} & \leqslant & t^{1 / q} 2^{di / p} \left[ \int_0^1 s^{- \gamma q} e^{- cq 2^{2 i} t (1 - s)} \mathrm{d} s \right]^{1 / q} \left[ \int_0^t s^{\gamma p} \| \Delta_i f (s)\|_{L^p}^p \mathrm{d} s \right]^{1 / p}\\ & \lesssim_{\gamma, q} & 2^{id / p} 2^{- 2 i / q} \left[ \int_0^t s^{\gamma p} \| \Delta_i f (s)\|_{L^p}^p \mathrm{d} s \right]^{1 / p} \end{eqnarray*}

which allows us to bound \(\| I f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}}\). In order to estimate \(\| t \mapsto t^{\gamma'} I f \|_{C^{\alpha / 2}_T L^{\infty}}\) we write

\begin{eqnarray*} \|t^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (t) - s^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (s)\|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \int_s^t v^{\gamma' - 1} \| \Delta_i u (v) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} v + | t - s | 2^{i (d + 2) / p} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)}\\ & & + \left\| \int_s^t v^{\gamma'} \Delta_i f (v) \mathrm{d} v \right\|_{L^{\infty}} \end{eqnarray*}

We can estimate the first term as

\(\displaystyle \int_s^t v^{\gamma' - 1} \| \Delta_i u (v) \|_{L^{\infty}} \mathrm{d} v \lesssim 2^{i (d + 2) / p} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \int_s^t v^{\gamma' - \gamma - 1} \mathrm{d} v.\)

For the third term we have

\begin{eqnarray*} \left\| \int_s^t v^{\gamma} \Delta_i f (v) \mathrm{d} v \right\|_{L^{\infty}} & \lesssim & \left[ \int_s^t \mathrm{d} v \right]^{1 / q} \left[ \int_s^t v^{\gamma p} \| \Delta_i f (s)\|_{L^{\infty}}^p \mathrm{d} v \right]^{1 / p}\\ & \lesssim & 2^{id / p} |t - s|^{1 / q} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \end{eqnarray*}

We obtain then if \(2^{2 i} | t - s | \leqslant 1\)

\(\displaystyle \|t^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (t) - s^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (s)\|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim 2^{id / p} | t - s |^{1 / q} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)}\)

and if \(2^{2 i} | t - s | > 1\) we just use the trivial estimate

\(\displaystyle \|t^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (t) - s^{\gamma'} \Delta_i u (s)\|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim 2^{id / p} 2^{- 2 i / q} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} \lesssim 2^{id / p} | t - s |^{1 / q} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} .\)

Therefore, for every \(\kappa \in [0, 1]\):

\(\displaystyle {\|t^{\gamma}}' \Delta_i {u (t) - s^{\gamma}}' \Delta_i u (s)\|_{L^{\infty}} \lesssim 2^{(\frac{d + 2}{p} - 2) i} 2^{2 \kappa i / q} |t - s|^{\kappa / q} \| \Delta_i f \|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma, p}_T L^p (\mathbb{T}^3)} .\)

Choosing \(\kappa / q = \alpha / 2\) we obtain the desired estimate.\(\Box\)

Estimates on Bony's paraproducts and commutators

Lemma 23. For any \(\beta \in \mathbb{R}\) we have

\(\displaystyle \|f \prec g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} \lesssim_{\beta} \|f\|_{L^{\infty}} \|g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}},\) (66)

and for \(\alpha < 0\) furthermore

\(\displaystyle \|f \prec g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha + \beta}} \lesssim_{\alpha, \beta} \|f\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \|g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} .\) (67)

For \(\alpha + \beta > 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle \|f \circ g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha + \beta}} \lesssim_{\alpha, \beta} \|f\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \|g\|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} .\) (68)

A natural corollary is that the product \(fg\) of two elements \(f \in \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}\) and \(g \in \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}\) is well defined as soon as \(\alpha + \beta > 0\), and that it belongs to \(\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\gamma}\), where \(\gamma = \min \{\alpha, \beta, \alpha + \beta\}\). We will also need a commutator estimation:

Lemma 24. Let \(\alpha > 0\), \(\beta \in \mathbb{R}\), and let \(f, g \in \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}\), and \(h \in \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}\). Then

\(\displaystyle \| f \prec (g \prec h) - (f g) \prec h \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha + \beta}} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \| g \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \| h \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} .\)

We collect in the following lemma various estimates for the modified paraproduct \(f \prec\!\!\!\prec g\), proofs are again in [7].

Lemma 25.

  1. For any \(\beta \in \mathbb{R}\) and \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\) we have

    \(\displaystyle t^{\gamma} \|f \prec\!\!\!\prec g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} \lesssim \|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_t L^{\infty}} \|g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}},\) (69)

    for all \(t > 0\), and for \(\alpha < 0\) furthermore

    \(\displaystyle t^{\gamma} \|f \prec\!\!\!\prec g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha + \beta}} \lesssim \|f\|_{\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}_t \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} \|g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}} .\) (70)

  2. Let \(\alpha, \delta \in (0, 2)\), \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\), \(T > 0\), and let \(f \in \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}_T^{\gamma, \delta}\), \(g \in C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}\), and \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} g \in C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - 2}\). Then

    \(\displaystyle \| f \prec\!\!\!\prec g \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_T} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \delta}_T} \left( \| g \|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha}} + \left\| \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} g \right\|_{C_T \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha - 2}} \right) . \) (71)

We introduce various commutators which allow to control non-linear functions of paraproducts and also the interaction of the paraproducts with the heat kernel.

Lemma 26.

  1. For \(\alpha, \beta, \gamma \in \mathbb{R}\) such that \(\alpha + \beta + \gamma > 0\) and \(\alpha \in (0, 1)\) there exists bounded trilinear maps

    \(\displaystyle \operatorname{com}_1, \overline{\operatorname{com}}_1 : \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha} \times \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta} \times \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\gamma} \rightarrow \mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\alpha + \beta + \gamma},\)

    such that for smooth \(f, g, h\) they satisfy

    \(\displaystyle \operatorname{com}_1 (f, g, h) = (f \prec g) \circ h - f (g \circ h) . \) (72)
    \(\displaystyle \overline{\operatorname{com}}_1 (f, g, h) = (f \prec\!\!\!\prec g) \circ h - f (g \circ h) . \) (73)

  2. Let \(\alpha \in (0, 2)\), \(\beta \in \mathbb{R}\), and \(\gamma \in [0, 1)\). Then the bilinear maps

    \(\displaystyle \operatorname{com}_2 (f, g) : = f \prec g - f \prec\!\!\!\prec g. \) (74)
    \(\displaystyle \operatorname{com}_3 (f, g) : = \left[ \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}, f \prec\!\!\!\prec \right] g := \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} (f \prec\!\!\!\prec g) - f \prec\!\!\!\prec \mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} g. \) (75)

    have the bounds

    \(\displaystyle t^{\gamma} \| \operatorname{com}_2 (f, g) (t) \|_{\alpha + \beta} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_t} \| g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}}, \qquad t > 0. \) (76)

    as well as

    \(\displaystyle t^{\gamma} \| \operatorname{com}_3 (f, g) (t) \|_{\alpha + \beta - 2} \lesssim \| f \|_{\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em}^{\gamma, \alpha}_t} \| g (t) \|_{\mathscr{C} \hspace{.1em}^{\beta}}, \qquad t > 0. \) (77)

Proofs can be found in [7].

A.3Estimation of finite-chaos diagrams

In this section we recall a few well-known results on the estimation of finite chaos diagrams. For additional details see Chapter 10 of [8] and [18]. First of all we need to characterize the local behaviour of the heat kernel \(P_t (x)\) and of the covariance \(C_{\varepsilon} (t, x)\) of the Gaussian field \(Y_{\varepsilon}\).

Remark 27. We sometimes use a slightly different version of the heat kernel, namely

\(\displaystyle \check{P}_t (x) = \frac{1}{(4 \pi t)^{3 / 2}} e^{- \frac{| x |^2}{4 t}} e^{- t} \mathbb{1}_{t \geqslant 0}\)

in order to have that \(X (t, x) = \int_{- \infty}^t \int_{\mathbb{T}^3} P_{t - s} (x - y) v (s, y) \mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d} y\) is the stationary solution to \(\mathscr{L} \hspace{.2em} X = - X + v\). However, the bounds on the covariance \(C_{\varepsilon}\) remain trivially valid in this setting.

Lemma 28. The heat kernel \(P (\zeta) := P (t, x) = \frac{1}{(4 \pi t)^{3 / 2}} e^{- \frac{| x |^2}{4 t}} \mathbb{I}_{t \geqslant 0}\) has the bound

\(\displaystyle | P (\zeta) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- 3} .\)

Let \(k \in \mathbb{N}^4\) a multi-index with \(| k | = 2 k_1 + k_2 + \cdots + k_4\). Then for every multi-index \(| k | \leqslant 2\) we have:

\(\displaystyle | D^k P_t (x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- 3 - | k |} .\)

Proof.

\(\displaystyle | P_t (x) | (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^3 \lesssim [1 + (| x | | t |^{- 1 / 2})^3] e^{- \frac{| x |^2}{4 | t |}} = \left( 1 + \left| \alpha \right|^3 \right) e^{- \frac{| \alpha |}{4}} < + \infty\)

In the same way we prove that \(| \partial_t P_t (x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^5\), \(| \partial_{x_i} P_t (x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^4\) and \(| \partial_{x_i} \partial_{x_j} P_t (x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^5\).\(\Box\)

We recall a special case of Lemma 10.14 of [8], which is enough for our purpose. We use the notation \(\unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980} := (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)\) for \(\zeta = (t, x) \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\).

Lemma 29. Let \(f, g : \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3 \setminus \{0\} \rightarrow \mathbb{R}\) smooth, integrable at infinity and such that \(|f (\zeta) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\alpha}\) and \(|g (\zeta) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\beta}\) in a ball \(B = \{\zeta \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3 : \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}< 1, \zeta \neq 0\}\). Then if \(\alpha, \beta \in (- 5, 0)\) and \(\alpha + \beta + 5 < 0\) we have

\(\displaystyle |f \ast g (\zeta) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\alpha + \beta + 5}\)

in a ball centered in the origin.

Moreover, if \(\alpha, \beta \in (- 5, 0)\) and \(0 < \alpha + \beta + 5 < 1\) and for every multi-index \(| k | \leqslant 2\) we have \(| \mathrm{D}^k f (\zeta) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\alpha - | k |}\) and \(| \mathrm{D}^k g (\zeta) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\beta - | k |}\), then

\(\displaystyle |f \ast g (\zeta) - f \ast g (0) | \lesssim \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}^{\alpha + \beta + 5}\)

in a ball centered in the origin.

Remark 30. The covariance \(C_{\varepsilon}\) of \(Y_{\varepsilon}\) can be written as \(C_{\varepsilon} = \check{P} \ast \check{C}_{\varepsilon} \ast \check{P} \) with \(\check{C}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) := \mathbb{E} (\eta_{\varepsilon} (t, x) \eta_{\varepsilon} (0, 0))\). Recall from the introduction that \(\check{C}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) = \varepsilon^{- 5} \tilde{C}^{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{- 2} t, \varepsilon^{- 1} x)\) where \(\)\(\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}\) is the covariance of the Gaussian process \(\eta\) defined on \(\mathbb{R} \times (\mathbb{T}/ \varepsilon)^3\), and \(\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon} (t - s, x - y) = \Sigma (t - s, x - y)\) if \(\operatorname{dist} (x, y) \leqslant 1\) and \(0\) otherwise (so that the family of functions \(\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}\) is bounded uniformly on \(\varepsilon\) by a \(C^{\infty}_c\) function). Then there exists a family of functions \(C_Y^{\varepsilon}\) defined on \(\mathbb{R} \times (\mathbb{T}/ \varepsilon)^3\) such that \(C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) = \varepsilon^{- 1} C_Y^{\varepsilon} (\varepsilon^{- 2} t, \varepsilon^{- 1} x)\) and \(C_Y^{\varepsilon} (t, x) = [\check{P} \ast \check{C}_{\varepsilon} \ast \check{P} ] (t, x)\).

Lemma 31. The covariance \(C_{\varepsilon}\) has the bound, for every multi-index \(| k | \leqslant 2\):

\(\displaystyle | D^k C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- 1 - | k |} .\)

Moreover, we have

\(\displaystyle \varepsilon^{| k | + 1} | D^k C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | \lesssim 1\)

Proof. The first bound is obtained directly from Lemma 28 and Lemma 29. Indeed, since by hypothesis \(\tilde{C}^{\varepsilon}\) has compact support, it is easy to see that \(| \check{C}_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | \lesssim (| t |^{1 / 2} + | x |)^{- 5}\). The second bound is obtained by a simple change of variables in the convolution defining \(C_{\varepsilon}\). \(\Box\)

Lemma 32. We have \(\int_{s, x} P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 \lesssim | \log \varepsilon |\) and for every \(n \geqslant 3\) \(\varepsilon^{n - 2} \int_{s, x} P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^n \lesssim 1\).

Proof. From the fact that \(P_{\varepsilon^2 s} (\varepsilon x) = \varepsilon^{- 3} P_s (x)\) together with Remark 30 we obtain

\(\displaystyle \int_{\mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3} P_s (x) [C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 \mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d} x \lesssim \int_{B (0, \varepsilon^{- 1})} P_s (x) [C_Y^{\varepsilon} (s, x)]^2 \mathrm{d} s \mathrm{d} x \lesssim | \log \varepsilon |\)

with \(B (0, R) = \{\zeta \in \mathbb{R}^4 : \unicode{x2980} \zeta \unicode{x2980}< R, \zeta \neq 0\}\) a “parabolic” ball centered in the origin. The second estimation is obtained in the same way.\(\Box\)

Lemma 33. For \(m \in (0, 3)\), \(n \in (3, 5)\), define for \(\zeta, \zeta' \in \mathbb{R} \times \mathbb{T}^3\)

\(\displaystyle I_m := \int | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta') |^m | \mu_{q, \zeta} \mu_{q, \zeta'} |, \qquad \tilde{I}_n := \int | C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta - \zeta') |^n | \tilde{\mu}_{q, \zeta} \tilde{\mu}_{q, \zeta'} |\)

with \(\mu_{q, \zeta} := K_{q, \bar{x}} (y) \delta (t - s) \mathrm{d} \zeta\) and \(\tilde{\mu}_{q, \zeta} := \left[ \int_x K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) P_{t - s} (x - y) \right] d \zeta\) for \(\zeta = (s, y)\). Then

\(\displaystyle I_m \lesssim 2^{mq} \qquad \text{and} \qquad \tilde{I}_n \lesssim 2^{(n - 4) q} .\)

Proof. The estimation of \(I_m\) is easily obtained by Lemma 31 and a change of variables. For \(\tilde{I}_n\) observe that for every \(q > 0\)

\(\displaystyle \tilde{\mu}_{q, \zeta} = [\int_x K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) (P_{t - s} (x - y) - P_{t - s} (\bar{x} - y))] d \zeta\)

and then we can apply Lemma 29 to obtain the result.\(\Box\)

Lemma 34. We have for every \(\sigma \in [0, 1]\)

\(\displaystyle \sup_{x \in \mathbb{T}^3} | C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) - C_{\varepsilon} (0, x) | \lesssim \varepsilon^{- 1 - 2 \sigma} | t |^{\sigma}\)

Proof. It is easy to obtain by interpolation knowing that \(| \partial_t C_{\varepsilon} (t, x) | \lesssim \varepsilon^{- 3}\) from Lemma 31.\(\Box\)

Lemma 35. We have for every \(\alpha < 3\)

\(\displaystyle \sum_{i \sim j} \left| \int K_i (x - y) P_t (y) \mathrm{d} y \right| \int \frac{| K_j (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim \frac{1}{(| x | + t^{1 / 2})^{3 + \alpha}}\)

Proof. We will show that

\(\displaystyle \left| \int K_i (x - y) P_t (y) \mathrm{d} y \right| \lesssim 2^{- i} (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- 4}, \) (78)

and that

\(\displaystyle \int \frac{| K_i (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- \alpha}, \) (79)

from which we deduce that

\(\displaystyle \sum_{i \sim j} \left| \int K_i (x - y) P_t (y) \mathrm{d} y \right| \int \frac{| K_j (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim \sum_i \frac{2^{- i}}{(| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{4 + \alpha}} .\)

Bounding the sum over \(i\) with an integral, we conclude

\(\displaystyle \int_0^1 \frac{\mathrm{d} \lambda}{\lambda} \frac{\lambda}{(| x | + t^{1 / 2} + \lambda)^{4 + \alpha}} = \frac{1}{(| x | + t^{1 / 2})^{3 + \alpha}} \int_0^{1 / (| x | + t^{1 / 2})} \frac{\mathrm{d} \lambda}{(1 + \lambda)^{4 + \alpha}} \lesssim \frac{1}{(| x | + t^{1 / 2})^{3 + \alpha}} .\)

Let us show (78). We want to estimate

\begin{eqnarray*} I & = & \int K_i (x - y) P_t (y) \mathrm{d} y = \int K_i (x - y) [P_t (y) - P_t (x)] \mathrm{d} y\\ & = & \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau \int K_i (x - y) [P_t' (x + \tau (y - x)) (y - x)] \mathrm{d} y\\ | I | & \lesssim & \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau \int | (y - x) K_i (x - y) | | P_t' (x + \tau (y - x)) | \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau \int | y K_1 (y) | | P_t' (x + \tau 2^{- i} y) | \mathrm{d} y\\ & \lesssim & 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau \int | y K_1 (y) | \frac{e^{- c | x + \tau 2^{- i} y |^2 / t}}{t^2} \mathrm{d} y \end{eqnarray*}

where

\(\displaystyle | P'_t (z) | = \left| C \frac{e^{- | z |^2 / t}}{t^{4 / 2}} \frac{z}{t^{1 / 2}} \right| \leqslant C \frac{e^{- c | z |^2 / t}}{t^2} .\)

When \(t^{1 / 2} \geqslant 2^{- i}, | x |\) we have

\(\displaystyle | I | \lesssim 2^{- i} t^{- 2} \lesssim 2^{- i} (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- 4} .\)

When \(2^{- i} \geqslant t^{1 / 2}, | x |\) we estimate simply

\(\displaystyle | I | \lesssim \int | K_i (x - y) | P_t (y) \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{3 i} \lesssim 2^{- i} (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- 4} .\)

When \(| x | \geqslant 2^{- i}, t^{1 / 2}\) we have instead that either \(| x | \geqslant 2 \tau 2^{- i} | y |\) or \(| x | < 2 \tau 2^{- i} | y |\). In the first region \(| x + \tau 2^{- i} y | \geqslant c | x |\) so

\(\displaystyle | I | \lesssim 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau \int | y K_1 (y) | \frac{e^{- c' | x |^2 / t}}{t^2} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{- i} \frac{e^{- c' | x |^2 / t}}{t^2} \lesssim 2^{- i} | x |^{- 4} \lesssim 2^{- i} (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- 4} .\)

while in the second region \(| y | \geqslant 2^i | x | / (2 \tau)\), then \(\)\(| y K_1 (y) | \leqslant | y K_1 (y) |^{1 / 2} f (2^i | x | / (2 \tau))\) where \(f\) is a rapidly decreasing function and in this region

\begin{eqnarray*} | I | & \lesssim & 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau f (2^i | x | / (2 \tau)) \int \frac{e^{- c | x + \tau 2^{- i} y |^2 / t}}{t^2} \mathrm{d} y\\ & \lesssim & 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau f (2^i | x | / (2 \tau)) \int \frac{e^{- c' | \tau 2^{- i} y |^2 / t}}{t^{3 / 2} | \tau 2^{- i} y |} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{- i} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau f (2^i | x | / (2 \tau)) \frac{2^{3 i}}{\tau^3 | x |} \int \frac{e^{- c' | y |^2 / t}}{t^{3 / 2}} \mathrm{d} y\\ & \lesssim & \frac{2^{- i}}{| x |^4} \int_0^1 \mathrm{d} \tau f (2^i | x | / (2 \tau)) \frac{2^{3 i} | x |^3}{\tau^3} \lesssim \frac{2^{- i}}{| x |^4} \lesssim 2^{- i} (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- 4} . \end{eqnarray*}

So we conclude that (78) holds. Let us turn to (79). When \(t^{1 / 2} \geqslant 2^{- i}, | x |\) we have

\(\displaystyle \int \frac{| K_i (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim \frac{1}{t^{\alpha}} \int | K_i (x - y) | \mathrm{d} y \lesssim \frac{1}{t^{\alpha}} \lesssim (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- \alpha} .\)

When \(2^{- i} \geqslant t^{1 / 2}, | x |\) we estimate

\(\displaystyle \int \frac{| K_i (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{\alpha i} \int \frac{| K_1 (y) |}{| 2^i x + y |^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{2 i} \sup_z \int \frac{| K_1 (y) |}{| z + y |^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{2 i} \lesssim (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- \alpha},\)

and finally when \(| x | \geqslant 2^{- i}, t^{1 / 2}\) we have either \(| x | \geqslant 2^{- i + 1} | y |\) or \(| x | < 2^{- i + 1} | y |\). In the first region \(| x + 2^{- i} y | \geqslant c | x |\) so

\(\displaystyle \int \frac{| K_i (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim \int \frac{| K_1 (y) |}{| x + 2^{- i} y |^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim | x |^{- \alpha} \lesssim (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- \alpha},\)

while in the second \(| y | \geqslant 2^i | x | / 2\), then \(\)\(| K_1 (y) | \leqslant | K_1 (y) |^{1 / 2} f (2^i | x | / 2)\) where \(f\) is another rapidly decreasing function and in this region

\(\displaystyle \int \frac{| K_i (x - y) |}{(| y | + t^{1 / 2})^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim f (2^i | x | / 2) \int \frac{| K_1 (y) |^{1 / 2}}{| 2^{- i} y |^{\alpha}} \mathrm{d} y \lesssim 2^{\alpha i} f (2^i | x | / 2) \lesssim | x |^{- \alpha} \lesssim (| x | + t^{1 / 2} + 2^{- i})^{- \alpha},\)

concluding our argument.\(\Box\)

Lemma 36. For \(m, n \in (0, 5)\), \(k, \ell \in [0, 2)\) define

\(\displaystyle I_{k, m, n} := \int_{\zeta_{1, 2}, \zeta'_{1, 2}} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^k C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^{\ell} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta'_1)^m C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_2 - \zeta'_2)^n | \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \mu_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |,\)

with \(\mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2}\) for \(\bar{\zeta} = (t, \bar{x})\), \(\zeta_i = (s_i, x_i)\) \(i = 1, 2\) defined as

\(\displaystyle \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} := [\int_{x, y} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) \sum_{i \sim j} K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2 .\)

If \(\ell = 0\), \(0 < m + k - 2 < 5\), \(m + k - 2 \in (- 1, 5)\) and \(k + m + n - 4 \in (0, 5)\) we have the bound

\(\displaystyle I_{k, m, n} \lesssim 2^{(k + m + n - 4) q} .\)

If \((k + m - 2), (\ell + m - 2) \in (0, 5)\), \(k + m + \ell - 4 \in (0, 5)\) and \(k + \ell + m + n - 4 \in (0, 5)\) we have the bound \(\)

\(\displaystyle I_{k, m, n} \lesssim 2^{(k + \ell + m + n - 4) q} .\)

Proof. Observe that

\begin{eqnarray*} \mu_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} & = & [\sum_{i \sim j} \int_{x, y} K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2) K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) (P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1) - P_{t - s_1} (\bar{x} - x_1))] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\\ & & + [\sum_{i \sim j} \int_{x, y} (K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) - K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2)) K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\\ & = & K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2) [P_{t - s_1} (x_2 - x_1) - P_{t - s_1} (\bar{x} - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\\ & & + [\sum_{i \sim j} \int_{x, y} [K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) - K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2)] K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\\ & = & \bar{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} + \hat{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} \end{eqnarray*}

where in the first line we used \(\int_y K_{i, x} (y) = 0\) and the fact that \(\int_x K_{i, x} (x_1') K_{j, x} (x_2) = 0\) if \(| i - j | > 1\) and \(\sum_{i, j} K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) = \delta (x_2 - y) \delta (x_2 - x)\). Now the estimation of the term

\(\displaystyle \bar{I}_{k, m, n} := \int_{\zeta_{1, 2}, \zeta'_{1, 2}} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^k C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1' - \zeta'_2)^{\ell} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta'_1)^m C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^n | \bar{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \bar{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |,\)

with \(\bar{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2) [P_{t - s_1} (x_2 - x_1) - P_{t - s_1} (\bar{x} - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\) can be done with Lemma 29 and gives the expected result. The integral

\(\displaystyle \hat{I}_{k, m, n} := \int_{\zeta_{1, 2}, \zeta'_{1, 2}} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^k C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1' - \zeta'_2)^{\ell} C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta'_1)^m C_{\varepsilon} (\zeta_1 - \zeta_2)^n | \hat{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} | | \hat{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1', \zeta_2'} |,\)

with \(\hat{\mu}_{q, \zeta_1, \zeta_2} = [\sum_{i \sim j} \int_{x, y} [K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) - K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2)] K_{i, x} (y) K_{j, x} (x_2) P_{t - s_1} (y - x_1)] \delta (t - s_2) \mathrm{d} \zeta_1 \mathrm{d} \zeta_2\) can be estimated by multiple changes of variables. We have \(K_{q, \bar{x}} (x) - K_{q, \bar{x}} (x_2) = 2^{3 q} (x_2 - x) \int_0^1 K' (2^q (x_2 - x) \tau - 2^q (\bar{x} - x_2)) \mathrm{d} \tau\), and by the scaling properties of \(C_{\varepsilon}\) and \(P_{t, y}\), namely \(C_{\varepsilon} (2^{- 2 i} s, 2^{- i} x) \lesssim 2^i C_{\varepsilon} (s, x)\) and \(P_{2^{- 2 i} s} (2^{- i} x) \lesssim 2^{3 i} P_s (x)\) given by Lemma 28 and Lemma 31, we obtain easily the bound on \(\)\(\hat{I}_{k, m, n}\) by rescaling the integral.\(\Box\)

Appendix BSome Malliavin calculus

We recall here some tools from Malliavin calculus that are widely used in the rest of the paper. An introduction to Malliavin calculus and the proofs of some results of this Appendix can be found in [20, 19, 24]. Lemma 43 was inspired by the calculations of [25].

B.1Notation

Let \(\{ W (h) \}_{h \in H}\) be an isonormal Gaussian process indexed by a real separable Hilbert space \(H\). Let \((\Omega, \mathcal{F}, \mathbb{P})\) a probability space with \(\mathcal{F}\) generated by the isonormal Gaussian process \(W\), we note \(L^2 (\Omega) = \bigoplus_{n \geqslant 0} \mathcal{H}_n\) the well-known Wiener chaos decomposition of \(L^2 (\Omega)\). For any real separable Hilbert space \(V\) and \(k \in \mathbb{N}\), let \(\)\(\mathrm{D}^k : L^2 (\Omega ; V) \rightarrow L^2 (\Omega ; H^{\odot k} \otimes V)\) be the Malliavin derivative and \(\delta^k : L^2 (\Omega ; H^{\otimes k} \otimes V) \rightarrow L^2 (\Omega ; V)\) the divergence operator (also called Skorohod integral) defined as the adjoint of \(\mathrm{D}^k\). For \(p \geqslant 1\) we will write \(\mathbb{D}^{k, p} (V) \subset L^p (\Omega ; V)\) for the closure of smooth random variables with respect to the norm

\(\displaystyle \| \Psi \|_{\mathbb{D}^{k, p} (V)} = [\mathbb{E} (\| \Psi \|_V^p) + \sum_{j = 1}^k \mathbb{E} (\| \mathrm{D}^j \Psi \|^p_{H^{\otimes j} \otimes V})]^{1 / p}\)

with the notation \(\mathbb{D}^{k, p} := \mathbb{D}^{k, p} (\mathbb{R})\). Let \(\{ P_t \}_{t \in \mathbb{R}^+}\) the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck semigroup and \(L : L^2 (\Omega) \rightarrow L^2 (\Omega)\) its generator (i.e. \(e^{tL} = P_t\)). Following [24] we introduce the Green operator

\(\displaystyle G_j = (j - L)^{- 1}\)

with the notation

\(\displaystyle G_{[j]}^{[m]} := \prod_{k = j}^m G_k \quad \text{for} \quad 1 \leqslant j \leqslant m \) (80)

so that \(G_{[j]}^{[j]} = G_j\). To avoid confusion, it is worth stressing that \(G_{[j]}^{[m]}\) is not the \(m\)-th power of the operator \(G_j\) but just a shortcut for \(\prod_{k = j}^m G_k \).

B.2Partial chaos expansion

Let \(\Psi \in L^2 (\Omega)\) which has the Wiener chaos decomposition \(\Psi = \sum_{n \geqslant 0} J_n \Psi\). Then by Proposition 1.2.2 of [20] \(\mathrm{D} J_n \Psi = J_{n - 1} \mathrm{D} \Psi\), and knowing that \(LJ_n \Psi = - nJ_n \Psi\) we obtain the commutation property

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- \alpha} \Psi = \mathrm{D} \sum_{n \geqslant 0} \frac{1}{(j + n)^{\alpha}} J_n \Psi = \sum_{n \geqslant 1} \frac{1}{(j + n)^{\alpha}} J_{n - 1} \mathrm{D} \Psi = (j + 1 - L)^{- \alpha} \mathrm{D} \Psi \) (81)

for every \(\alpha > 0\), \(j > 0\). The above formula holds also for \(j = 0\) if \(\mathbb{E} (\Psi) = 0\).

The results we have recalled so far let us write an \(n\)th-order Wiener chaos expansion for a random variable in \(\mathbb{D}^{n, 2}\):

Lemma 37. Let \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{n, 2}\) and \(G_{[j]}^{[m]}\) as in (80). Then for every \(n \in \mathbb{N}\backslash \{ 0 \}\) \(G_{[1]}^{[n]} \mathrm{D}^n \Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^n\), \(J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \hspace{1em} \delta^k\) \(\forall 0 \leqslant k < n\) and

\(\displaystyle \delta^n G_{[1]}^{[n]} \mathrm{D}^n \Psi = (\operatorname{id}- J_0 - \ldots - J_{n - 1}) \Psi = \Psi - \sum_{k = 0}^{n - 1} \frac{1}{k!} \delta^k J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi . \) (82)

\(\displaystyle \)

Proof. We have for any \(\Psi \in L^2 (\Omega)\), since \(L = - \delta \mathrm{D}\) ([20], Proposition 1.4.3):

\(\displaystyle \Psi -\mathbb{E} (\Psi) = L L^{- 1} (\Psi - J_0 \Psi) = - \delta \mathrm{D} L^{- 1} (\Psi - J_0 \Psi) = \delta (1 - L)^{- 1} \mathrm{D} \Psi\)

where we used (81), and the fact that \((1 - L)^{- 1} \mathrm{D} \Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta\) is obvious by construction. This yields the first order expansion \(\Psi =\mathbb{E} (\Psi) + \delta (1 - L)^{- 1} \mathrm{D} \Psi\). Iterating the expansion up to order \(n\) we obtain (82). It is clear that \(J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \hspace{1em} \delta^k\) since \(J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi\) is constant with values in \(H^{\otimes k}\). The second equality comes from the fact that \(\delta^k J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi \in \mathcal{H}_k\) \(\forall k \in \mathbb{N}\), indeed \(\forall \Psi' \in \mathbb{D}^{k, 2}\):

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} (\delta^k (J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi) J_k \Psi') = \langle J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi, J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi' \rangle_{L^2 (\Omega ; H^{\otimes k})} =\mathbb{E} (\delta^k (J_0 \mathrm{D}^k \Psi) \Psi')\)
\(\Box\)

In order to obtain \(L^p\) estimations of the remainder term \(\delta^n G_{[1]}^{[n]} \mathrm{D}^n \Psi\) generated by expansion (82), we used the following lemmas:

Lemma 38. ([20], Prop. 1.5.7) Let \(V\) be a real separable Hilbert space. For every \(p > 1\) and every \(q \in \mathbb{N}, k \geqslant q\) and every \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{k, p} (H^q \otimes V)\) we have

\(\displaystyle \| \delta^q (\Psi) \|_{\mathbb{D}^{k - q, p} (V)} \lesssim_{k, p} \| \Psi \|_{\mathbb{D}^{k, p} (H^q \otimes V)}\)

Remark 39. Let \(V\) be a real separable Hilbert space. For every \(v \in V\) and every \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (H^{\otimes q})\) with \(q \in \mathbb{N}\) we have \(\Psi \otimes v \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^q\) and

\(\displaystyle \delta^q (\Psi) v = \delta^q (\Psi \otimes v) .\)

Indeed, notice that for every smooth \(\Psi' \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (V)\) and every smooth \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (H^{\otimes q})\) we have

\(\displaystyle \mathbb{E} (\langle \delta^q (\Psi \otimes v), \Psi' \rangle_V) =\mathbb{E} (\langle \Psi \otimes v, \mathrm{D}^q \Psi' \rangle_{H^{\otimes q} \otimes V}) =\mathbb{E} (\langle \delta^q (\Psi) v, \Psi' \rangle_V) .\)

Now since \(\mathrm{D}^q (\Psi \otimes v) = \mathrm{D}^q \Psi \otimes v\) and \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (H^{\otimes q})\), we have \(\Psi \otimes v \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (H^{\otimes q} \otimes V)\). Lemma 38 yields the bound \(\| \delta^q (\Psi \otimes v) \|_{L^2 (V)} \lesssim \| \Psi \otimes v \|_{\mathbb{D}^{q, 2} (H^{\otimes q} \otimes V)}\) which allows to pass to the limit for \(\Psi'\) and \(\delta^q (\Psi \otimes v)\) in \(L^2 (V)\).

Lemma 40. ([24], Prop. 4.3) For every \(j > 0\) the operator \((j - L)^{- 1 / 2}\) is bounded in \(L^p\) for every \(1 \leqslant p < \infty\).

Lemma 41. Let \(j \in \mathbb{N}\backslash \{ 0 \}\) and \(V\) a real separable Hilbert space. There exists a finite constant \(c_p\) such that for every \(\Psi \in L^p (\Omega, V)\):

\(\displaystyle \| \mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- 1 / 2} \Psi \|_{L^p (\Omega, H \otimes V)} \leqslant c_p \| \Psi \|_{L^p (\Omega, V)}\)

(where the operator \(\mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- 1 / 2}\) is defined on every \(\Psi\) which is polynomial in \(W (h_1), \ldots, W (h_n)\) and can be extended by density on \(L^p\)).

Proof. First notice that we can suppose w.l.o.g. \(\mathbb{E} (\Psi) = 0\) thanks to (81). Therefore we can write \(\mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- \frac{1}{2}}\) as

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- 1 / 2} = \mathrm{D} (- C)^{- 1} (- C) (j - L)^{- 1 / 2}\)

with \(C = - \sqrt{- L}\). We decompose the second part as \(- C (j - L)^{- 1 / 2} \Psi = \sum_{n = 1}^{\infty} \left( \frac{n}{j + n} \right)^{1 / 2} J_n \Psi = T_{\phi} \Psi\), with \(T_{\phi} \Psi := \sum_{n = 0}^{\infty} \phi (n) J_n \Psi\). We apply Theorem 1.4.2 of [20] to show that \(T_{\phi}\) is bounded in \(L^p\), indeed \(\phi (n) = h (1 / n)\) and \(h (x) = (jx + 1)^{- 1 / 2}\) which is analytic in a neighbourhood of 0. Finally, we can apply Proposition 1.5.2 of [20] to show that \(\mathrm{D} C^{- 1}\) is bounded in \(L^p\), thus concluding the proof.\(\Box\)

The two lemmas above give the following immediate corollary:

Corollary 42. For every \(1 \leqslant m \leqslant n\) the operator \(G_{[m]}^{[n]} := \prod_{j = m}^n (j - L)^{- 1}\) is bounded in \(L^p\) for every \(1 \leqslant p < \infty\).

Moreover, Let \(j \in \mathbb{N}\backslash \{ 0 \}\) and \(V\) a real separable Hilbert space. Then for every \(\Psi \in L^p (\Omega, V)\) we have:

\(\displaystyle \| \mathrm{D} (j - L)^{- 1} \Psi \|_{L^p (\Omega, H \otimes V)} \lesssim \| \Psi \|_{L^p (\Omega, V)} .\)

Moreover, for every \(0 \leqslant k \leqslant 2 m\), \(i \geqslant 0\) \(\)we have

\(\displaystyle \left\| {\mathrm{D}^k} G_{[i + 1]}^{[i + m]} \Psi \right\|_{L^p (\Omega, H^{\otimes k} \otimes V)} \lesssim \| \Psi \|_{L^p (\Omega, V)} .\)

The next lemma is one of the most useful tools of this paper. It allows us to write products of decompositions of the type (82) as sums of iterated Skorohod integrals. From now on we will note \(\langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}\) the \(r\)-th contraction, which to avoid inconsistency has to be taken between symmetric tensors. We also note \(h^{\odot n}_{v_1, \ldots, v_n} := h_{v_1} \odot \ldots \odot h_{v_n}\) for \(h_{v_1}, \ldots, h_{v_n} \in H\).

Lemma 43.

Let \(u = f (W (h_u)) h^{\otimes m}_u\) and \(v = Fh^{\odot n}_{v_1, \ldots, v_n}\) with \(f \in C^{m + n} (\mathbb{R})\) and \(F \in \mathbb{D}^{m + n, 2}\). Then

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) =\)

\(\displaystyle = \sum_{(q, r, i) \in I_{m, n}} C_{m, n, q, r, i} \delta^{m + n - q - r} [f^{(r - i)} (W (h_u)) \langle h_u^{\otimes m - i}, \mathrm{D}^{q - i} F \rangle_{H^{\otimes q - i}} \langle h^{\otimes r}_u, h^{\odot n}_{v_1, \ldots, v_n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}] \) (83)

with \(C_{m, n, q, r, i} := \binom{m}{q} \binom{n}{r} \binom{q}{i} \binom{r}{i} i!\) and \(I_{m, n} := \{ (q, r, i) \in \mathbb{N}^3 : 0 \leqslant q \leqslant m, 0 \leqslant r \leqslant n, 0 \leqslant i \leqslant q \wedge r \}\).

A trivial change of variables gives also:

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) =\)

\(\displaystyle = \sum_{(i, q, r) \in I'_{m, n}} C_{m, n, q + i, r + i, i} \delta^{m + n - q - r - 2 i} [f^{(r)} (W (h_u)) \langle h_u^{\otimes m - i}, \mathrm{D}^q F \rangle_{H^{\otimes q}} \langle h^{\otimes r + i}_u, h^{\odot n}_{v_1, \ldots, v_n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes r + i}}]\) (84)

with \(I'_{m, n} := \{ (i, q, r) \in \mathbb{N}^3 : 0 \leqslant i \leqslant m \wedge n, 0 \leqslant q \leqslant m - i, 0 \leqslant r \leqslant n - i \}\).

Remark 44. In the special case \(v = g (W (h_v)) h^{\otimes n}_v\) eq. (83) takes the form

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) = \sum_{(q, r, i) \in I_{m, n}} C_{m, n, q, r, i} \delta^{m + n - q - r} (\langle \mathrm{D}^{r - i} u, \mathrm{D}^{q - i} v \rangle_{H^{\otimes q + r - i}}) \) (85)

which is just a generalization to Skorohod integrals of the multiplication formula for multiple Wiener integrals ([23],[26]). We can write the above formula more explicitly as

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) = \sum_{(q, r, i) \in I_{m, n}} C_{m, n, q, r, i} \delta^{m + n - q - r} [f^{(r - i)} (W (h_u)) g^{(q - i)} (W (h_v)) h_u^{\otimes m - q} \otimes h_v^{\otimes n - r}] \langle h_u, h_v \rangle^{q + r - i} .\)

Remark 45. Note that one can assume w.l.o.g. the argument of \(\delta^{m + n - q - r}\) in (83) to be symmetric, and this would allow to iterate Lemma 43.

Remark 46. We can give the following intuition for the second formula in Lemma 43. The random variables \(u\) and \(v\) have an infinite chaos decomposition, and following the tree-like notation of [6] or [8] they could be thought of as having an infinite number of leaves which need to be contracted with each other.

It is apparent that the index \(i\) in the second equation denotes contractions between the already existing leaves of the trees \(u, v\). The indexes \(r\) and \(q\) count new leaves in each vertex that are created by the Malliavin derivatives, which are then contracted with other leaves from the other tree. There are then \(m + n - r - q - 2 i\) overall unmatched leaves which are arguments to the iterated Skorokhod integral.

The more intuitive interpretation of the second equation in Lemma 43 is the reason why we gave two distinct expression for the same quantity. Nevertheless, the formula (83) is more practical in the calculations and is more widely used throughout the paper.

Proof. (Lemma 43) Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Lemma 38 we can show that \(\langle D^r \delta^n (v), \delta^j (u) \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}} \in L^2 (\Omega, H^{\otimes m - j - r})\) for every \(0 \leqslant r + j \leqslant m\). \(\)Then we apply Lemma 47 to get:

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) = \sum_{r = 0}^n \binom{n}{r} \delta^{n - r} (\langle \mathrm{D}^r \delta^m (u), v \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}) .\)

Using the commutation formula (86) we rewrite the r.h.s. as

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) = \sum_{r = 0}^n \binom{n}{r} \sum_{i = 0}^{r \wedge m} \binom{r}{i} \binom{m}{i} i! \delta^{n - r} (\langle \delta^{m - i} (\mathrm{D}^{r - i} u), v \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}) .\)

We obtain

\(\displaystyle \langle \delta^{m - i} (\mathrm{D}^{r - i} u), v \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}} = \delta^{m - i} (f^{(r - i)} (W (h_u)) h_u^{\otimes m - i}) F \langle h^{\otimes r}_u, h^{\odot n}_{v_1, \ldots, v_n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}\)

and using again Lemma 47 we obtain

\(\displaystyle \langle \delta^{m - i} (\mathrm{D}^{r - i} u), v \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}} = \sum_{\ell = 0}^{m - i} \binom{m - i}{\ell} \delta^{m - i - \ell} (f^{(r - i)} (W (h_u)) \langle h_u^{\otimes m - i}, \mathrm{D}^{\ell} F \rangle_{H^{\otimes \ell}} \langle h^{\otimes r}_u, h_{v_1} \odot \ldots \odot h_{v_n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}})\)

where we used \(\delta^k (\Psi) h^{\otimes n - r} = \delta^k (\Psi \otimes h^{\otimes n - r})\) for \(\Psi \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^k\), as seen in Remark 39. Substituting this expression into \(\delta^m (u) \delta^n (v)\) we get

\(\displaystyle \delta^m (u) \delta^n (v) = \sum_{(r, i, \ell) \in J_{m, n}} A_{m, n, r, i, \ell} \delta^{m + n - r - i - \ell} [f^{(r - i)} (W (h_u)) \langle h_u^{\otimes m - i}, \mathrm{D}^{\ell} F \rangle_{H^{\otimes \ell}} \langle h^{\otimes r}_u, h_{v_1} \odot \ldots \odot h_{v_n} \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}]\)

where we set \(A_{m, n, r, i, \ell} := \binom{m}{i} \binom{n}{r} \binom{m - i}{\ell} \binom{r}{i} i!\) and \(J_{m, n} := \{ (r, i, \ell) \in \mathbb{N}^3 : 0 \leqslant r \leqslant n, 0 \leqslant i \leqslant r \wedge n, 0 \leqslant \ell \leqslant m - i \}\).

In order to complete the proof we just have to perform some basic changes of indexes. Taking \(q = \ell + i\) and noting that \(\binom{m}{i} \binom{m - i}{q - i} = \binom{q}{i} \binom{m}{q}\) we have \(A_{m, n, r, i, \ell} = \binom{m}{q} \binom{q}{i} \binom{n}{r} \binom{r}{i} i!\) and this yields (83).

Finally, we perform the change of variables \(q - i \rightarrow q\), \(r - i \rightarrow r\) to get the second formula.\(\Box\)

We give below the results we used to prove Lemma 43.

Lemma 47. ([25], Lemma 2.1) Let \(q \in \mathbb{N}\backslash \{ 0 \}\), \(\Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{q, 2}\), \(u \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^q\) and symmetric. Assume also that \(\forall 0 \leqslant r + j \leqslant q\) \(\langle D^r \Psi, \delta^j (u) \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}} \in L^2 (\Omega, H^{\otimes q - r - j})\). Then \(\forall 0 \leqslant r < q\) \(\langle D^r \Psi, u \rangle_r \in \operatorname{Dom} \delta^{q - r}\) and

\(\displaystyle \Psi \delta^q (u) = \sum_{r = 0}^q \binom{q}{r} \delta^{q - r} (\langle \mathrm{D}^r \Psi, u \rangle_{H^{\otimes r}}) .\)

Remark 48. Note that

\(\displaystyle \delta^n (h^{\otimes n}) = \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} W^n (h) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\)

where \(\mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} \cdot \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\) stands for the Wick product. Indeed \(\forall \Psi \in \mathbb{D}^{1, 2}\) we know that \(\mathbb{E} [\delta (h^{\otimes n}) \Psi] =\mathbb{E} [W (h) h^{\otimes n - 1} \Psi]\) using the definition of \(\delta\), and then \(\delta^n (h^{\otimes n}) = \delta^{n - 1} (W (h) h^{\otimes n - 1})\). From Lemma 47 we have, since \(\mathrm{D} W (h) = h\):

\(\displaystyle \delta^{n - 1} (W (h) h^{\otimes n - 1}) = \delta^{n - 1} (h^{\otimes n - 1}) W (h) - (n - 1) \langle h, h \rangle \delta^{n - 2} (h^{\otimes n - 2})\)

which gives by induction \(\delta^n (h^{\otimes n}) = \mathopen{\unicode{x27E6}} W^n (h) \mathclose{\unicode{x27E7}}\).

Lemma 49. Let \(j, k \in \mathbb{N}\), \(u \in \mathbb{D}^{j + k, 2} (H^{\otimes j})\) symmetric and such that all its derivatives are symmetric. We have

\(\displaystyle \mathrm{D}^k \delta^j (u) = \sum_{i = 0}^{k \wedge j} \binom{k}{i} \binom{j}{i} i! \delta^{j - i} (D^{k - i} u) \) (86)

Proof. If \(j = 0\), \(k = 1\) or \(k = 0, j = 1\) eq. (86) is trivial. Let \(j = k = 1\) and \(u \in \mathbb{D}^{2, 2} (H) \subset \mathbb{D}^{1, 2} (H)\). We can apply Proposition 1.3.2 of [20] to obtain \(\langle \mathrm{D} \delta (u), h \rangle = \langle u, h \rangle + \delta (\langle \mathrm{D} u, h \rangle)\) \(\forall h \in H\). Since by hypothesis \(\mathrm{D} u\) is symmetric we have \(\delta (\langle \mathrm{D} u, h \rangle) = \langle \delta \mathrm{D} u, h \rangle\), and then \(\mathrm{D} \delta (u) = u + \delta \mathrm{D} u\). The proof by induction is easy noticing that \(\mathrm{D} \delta^j = \delta^j \mathrm{D} + j \delta^{j - 1}\).\(\Box\)
